# MY PETITE STYLE

Product Requirements Document

Petite-first AI personal stylist for iPhone and iPad

Version 2.0 — consolidated master  |  Original draft September 26, 2026  |  Updated October 7, 2026

Primary design partner: Lily

## Document authority and navigation

**This v2 Markdown file is the authoritative master specification.** It supersedes [the preserved v0.1 Markdown snapshot](/Users/jramirez/Git/closet/My_Petite_Style_PRD_v0.1.md), including that snapshot's former authority statement. Maintain future requirements here. The handoff, competitor-review and try-on pointer files link here. Lily's feedback and its assessment are source/review artifacts, not independently authoritative requirements. The original Word file is also preserved as historical material. Document version 2.0 does not mean a second app release: V1/MVP below still means the first intended application release.

This workspace contains planning documents, not an implemented app. Documentation review does not establish feature quality, fit accuracy, wallet recovery, App Store approval or release readiness. All application acceptance and RV/TO checks are **Not run**. Current counts are 73 functional requirements, 103 acceptance criteria, 29 core RV checks and eight conditional TO checks.

**V2 decisions, October 3, 2026:** incorporate Lily's interview priorities and the owner's latest clarification. Petite means evidence-based suitability for her proportions, rather than a mandatory retailer label. Shopping aims for the closest documented fit/style match and preserves her choice and explicit refinement. Garments and shoes should resemble her real pieces as closely as the available source evidence and tested rendering allow; unknown details remain labeled. Every technically valid, permitted delivered preview is automatically retained in searchable private history, independent of Favorites. Ordinary swaps/reference replacement, dislike or subscription expiry do not delete that history. Explicit user-directed privacy/content deletion still applies. On Me is Lily's preferred, opt-in result mode and an early fidelity/cost feasibility target, not a claim of working full-outfit generation.

Core styling/wardrobe/recovery, multiple named Suitcases with a Settings default view, local unified search and private history/reuse requirements apply to V1. Main Closet includes all her clothing across suitcase memberships. Section 13 now specifies genuine AI web-search discovery and retailer links as the first-release Find One approach; catalog feeds and specialist fit vendors are later optional adapters, not launch dependencies. Enabled web shopping still needs its source/privacy/cost/quality evidence gate. The personal image service has independent TO gates; image packs apply only if images are enabled. Local boards remain independently useful and free, but a board-only build does not establish that Lily's preferred On Me experience is validated. If its image gates fail, show actual findings and obtain a reviewed product-scope decision before claiming her complete desired experience. Broader sizes/lengths and men's clothing remain later validation work within the same app foundation.

Decision confirmed October 3, 2026: the intended public audience is adults aged 18 or older, with initial App Store distribution in the United States. Keep the same app ready for separately reviewed country expansion; international shopping destinations are a distinct capability. See Sections 11.8 and 24.2.

**Product thesis**

Help petite women decide what to wear, confidently use what they already own, discover better combinations, and buy only the pieces they genuinely need.

## 1. Executive Summary

My Petite Style is a free-to-download iOS app with monthly subscription access to its AI stylist, designed as a petite-first personal stylist. A user chooses an occasion, reviews editable weather and season, sets optional today-only clothing comfort and required/preferred colors, and optionally names a starting piece. The normal target is three useful directions: Safe / Simple using her clothes, Stylish / Elevated using her clothes, and another Elevated owned look. When she explicitly enables a new-piece idea, the third direction may instead include a clearly unowned purchasable piece. A verified small wardrobe may yield one or two complete valid owned looks with an honest partial-result label; the app never fabricates clothing to fill three slots.

V1 is a universal iPhone and iPad application. iPad gets a styling workspace with comparative outfit views, a closet grid, and contextual editing panes that adapt to the actual window size. A moderated feedback board and optional, privacy-conscious improvement analytics support product development. App Store compliance, consent, entitlement validation, and server-enforced usage and spending controls are release requirements.

The app centers on styling the clothes she owns and keeping reusable looks. Every outfit is modular: select another real garment/color, mark Already Own, arrange a visual outfit board, and ask which occasions a look suits. Named Suitcases organize garment subsets for places/trips, with a remembered selectable source and Main Closet always covering all clothing. Named private collections and Favorites organize saved outfits; delivered personal previews remain searchable and revisitable with their exact source/outfit versions. The app learns gradually from editable, user-confirmed preferences without requiring a full closet upload. Lily's preferred opt-in On Me experience targets initial pictures of her wearing the selected looks, subject to Section 9.2's fidelity, privacy, latency and cost gates. A body photo is optional, and the free local outfit board remains an independent fallback.

Shopping is a secondary action when she still wants a missing piece after considering owned alternatives. Size and fit suitability are the primary shopping value: an attractive style, low price or preferred retailer never compensates for a known fit conflict. Find One uses genuine bounded web search and source-bearing retailer links, then compares product-specific evidence with her confirmed profile/fit references. Amazon, TikTok Shop, Target, Walmart and H&M form an editable initial preferred source pool; other permitted retailers remain available. Preferences can bias discovery and break ties, while stronger supported fit wins. Critical unknown size/length stays visibly unverified, not a recommended fit match. She chooses/buys at the retailer and explicitly confirms actual size/color/arrival, with optional completion reminders. No primary shopping feed, arbitrary one-country boundary, catalog/fit-vendor prerequisite or automatic gap checklist is planned. Actual access, source/fit quality, rights and costs still need Section 13 evidence.

Decision confirmed October 3, 2026: Lily's complete daily experience is the first design and validation priority: 4'11" height, approximately 110–130 lb as optional context, her confirmed measurements/garment fit, preferred styles and low-friction workflow. Broader adoption or profitability must not dilute that experience. Build flexible profile data and conditional fit rules from day one, then validate Lily first. Additional sizes, lengths and men's clothing are later coverage work, not a current universal-support claim. Keep one app; no separate mass-market app is planned. See Sections 3.3 and 10.2.

**Monetization decision**

Decision updated October 3, 2026: The app is free to download, with a monthly auto-renewable subscription for AI styling. The owner prioritizes Lily's experience and accepts her usage as a personal expense; profitability is not the primary goal. Selected users can receive complimentary ongoing access through Apple-issued offer codes for a separate non-consumable unlock. Public pricing is open for revision from the earlier US$4.99 plan, with US$9.99/month a candidate if needed, not a finalized price. Retain the one-month introductory trial for eligible users. If optional try-on ships, plan an included monthly image service allowance plus optional purchased image packs. Lily's owner-sponsored usage policy removes public allowance/credit-purchase requirements while retaining technical controls and a separately approved owner budget. See Section 14.

## 2. Product Vision and Principles

### 2.1 Vision

A petite woman should be able to open the app, tell it what she needs today, and quickly compare useful ways to dress without cataloging her entire closet or scrolling through hundreds of products. Three directions are the normal target, with honest valid partial results when inventory truly limits choice. Personal-photo rendering has a separately measured wait and must not be advertised as instant from the text-stylist target.

### 2.2 Product principles

- Closet first, shopping second. Primary navigation is Style Me, Closet, and Saved Looks. Reuse what the user owns and offer swaps before a deliberate Find One action; no shopping feed, automatic gap checklist, or image-generated purchase recommendation.
- Lily-first validation with flexible fit rules. Apply petite/short-proportion guidance where the supplied profile, confirmed garment fit and user preferences make it relevant. Lily's confirmed preferences take precedence over generic styling defaults; expansion must pass her regression checks.
- Options over answers. Target three distinct usable directions; accept a clearly labeled PartialWardrobe result containing one or two complete validated owned looks when complete-current-scope evidence verifies the limitation. A provider/shortlist failure is a different outcome. User choice and refinement govern the final look.
- Interactive, not static. Every visible outfit piece can be changed independently.
- Low setup burden. The wardrobe grows naturally through “Already Own,” optional photos, purchases, and feedback.
- Intentional discovery. Offer coordinated color combinations beyond Lily's habitual denim/black/same-family choices while respecting her chic, elegant, less trend-driven preferences. Briefly explain an unexpected pairing; a “Risky” identity is unnecessary.
- AI should feel invisible. Buttons and visual controls handle common actions; chat is available only when useful.
- Preferred pictures with an independent core. The separately enabled opt-in On Me mode targets personal previews for the selected directions. Ordinary boards use user photos and honest licensed representatives without runtime generation. An explicit Style Me/Generate Look/Update Preview action, disclosed image treatment and tested permission/budget authorize image work; scrolling, tapping an item, saving, reopening and navigation do not.
- Preserve exploration. Deliveries and earlier preview revisions remain revisitable until a reviewed user deletion or retained-content permission withdrawal applies. Favorite is organization, not a prerequisite to retaining a delivered preview; provider/cache expiry and undo bounds cannot silently erase local history.
- Use the available space. iPad supports comparison and editing together; narrow windows retain the same essential actions.
- Keep users in control of their data. Cloud AI permission, optional analytics, public feedback, and private iCloud sync are separate choices.
- Make paid service limits clear. Enforce cost protection on the server and disclose meaningful styling allowances before subscription activation.

## 3. Primary User and Problem

### 3.1 Primary design persona: Lily

| Attribute | Requirement / context |
| --- | --- |
| Body profile | Lily confirms 4'11" height. Inseam is unknown: she has not measured it, and the earlier owner-supplied 25/26-inch figure is not a confirmed body measurement or garment preference and must not drive fit rules. Optional 110–130 lb context is owner-supplied, never a fixed size mapping. Confirm other measurements/fit references only when useful; do not infer them from height, weight or a photo. |
| Primary need | Build flattering, stylish combinations from her clothes for nice occasions, casual outings, and everyday looking put together, while avoiding unnecessary purchases. |
| Setup preference | Does not want to photograph or catalog her entire wardrobe upfront. |
| Interaction preference | Quick option buttons plus an optional free-text starting point; chat should not dominate the interface. |
| Styling preference | Wants Safe / Simple and Stylish / Elevated alternatives with intentional coordinated color discovery: blue/light pink and olive/brick are positive examples. Her habitual black/denim/same-family colors are not an instruction to stay neutral. Chic, elegant, classy, cohesive and less trend-driven; feminine or sporty depends on occasion. Explicit monochrome remains supported. |
| Priority fit frustration | Pants are commonly too long, with inconsistent waist/seat/thigh fit; some jackets are too long and look oversized. Desired rise is around her navel with modest variation, avoiding low and extremely high rise. Evaluate sourced garment proportions, fit feedback, desired hem/shoe relationship and confirmed references; inseam and other unsupplied dimensions stay unknown. |
| Visual decision support | Prefers initial outfit options pictured on herself after opting in, with accurate or near-accurate selected garments and correct known colors. This is a gated appearance preview, not proof of physical fit. Honest representative details are needed for text-only garments; boards remain usable without a photo or image service. |
| Other comfort preferences | No firm sleeve-length complaint or fabric dislike has been confirmed. Keep these editable optional preferences; fabric can matter for occasion, weather and drape without imposing a general exclusion. |
| Discovery meaning | Wants interesting coordinated combinations rather than random/flashy choices or a fixed “Risky” label. Strong red/green is an example of an unappealing contrast, not a universal color prohibition. |
| Shopping preference | If she loves a look but is missing one item, she wants a direct path to find and order that item. |
| Planning behavior | Needs both urgent closet-only help and advance planning. Reuses successful looks by Work/Gym/Going Out collections; detailed wear chronology is not a priority. May use the app less often as her wardrobe becomes coherent. |

### 3.2 Problems to solve

- “I do not know what to wear for this occasion.”
- “I want help discovering my personal style.”
- “I own something similar - can I use that instead?”
- “I would never have thought those colors worked together.”
- “I do not want to buy another thing I effectively already own.”
- “Petite clothing is inconsistent across brands and regular sizing often fits poorly.”
- “I struggle to find pants with the right inseam, and want to see whether a trouser style works with the rest of my outfit.”
- “I want to plan ahead, but weather and season should still matter.”

### 3.3 Lily-first validation and staged expansion

Validate Lily's everyday styling, owned-piece swaps, occasion planning, saved looks, profile/fit feedback, recovery and sponsored access first on her iPhone 12. Optional shopping and try-on, if enabled, must also pass their own evidence gates using her confirmed needs and consented material. High quality means useful recommendations, accurate constraints, honest fit unknowns and recoverable work; physical fit and AI rendering are not guaranteed.

Keep profile/rule contracts adaptable without adding mandatory setup or presenting unsupported choices. There is no height, weight, size or gender eligibility cutoff, including no 5-foot maximum. Lily's measurements are her profile data, not application-wide constants. Initially validate her experience, then consider a small group with similar fit needs. Broader sizes/proportions and men's clothing require additional sizing, styling, catalog and any try-on evidence before claiming support. Existing core/recovery gates still apply to public release; one person's feedback does not establish population-wide fit quality. A separate app is a future product decision only if a substantially different experience becomes necessary.

## 4. Goals and Success Criteria

| Goal | MVP success signal |
| --- | --- |
| Fast styling | A first-time user can create a profile and generate a useful outfit without a wardrobe import. |
| Useful variety | Target Safe and distinct Elevated choices from eligible owned items, with a new-piece direction only when explicitly wanted. Verified one-/two-look wardrobe results are labeled partial; neither failure nor missing data invents ownership. |
| Modularity | Any major piece can be tapped and swapped without regenerating the entire experience from scratch. |
| Gradual learning | Marking “Already Own” immediately improves future recommendations. |
| Lily's fit and styling relevance | Recommendations respect her confirmed dimensions, fit references and preferences; consider petite/short variants where useful, preserve honest length unknowns, and pass her saved scenario suite before expansion or model/rule rollout. |
| Anti-overbuying | Shopping appears as an intentional action for missing pieces, not as the default output. |
| Weather awareness | Current or planned-date weather influences layers, shoes, fabric, and seasonality. |
| Subscription value | AI helps users find wearable looks and build a coherent wardrobe; useful saved-look reuse or lower later frequency can be success. Selected complimentary users receive equivalent styling access. Closet, preview history and backups remain accessible after expiry. |
| iPad usefulness | Users can compare outfit lanes and work with their closet in adaptive panes, including in a resized window. |
| Product improvement | Moderated ideas and privacy-minimal metrics reveal problems and useful styling outcomes without uploading closet contents for analytics. |

## 5. Core User Experience

### 5.1 Primary workflow

```text
FIRST LAUNCH
  -> Skippable personal fit profile + style preferences + optional budget

APP OPEN
  -> Restore remembered Main Closet / named Suitcase view; show active source
  -> Optional source switch; Settings can change the same saved preference
  -> Short affirmation
  -> Occasion buttons
  -> Weather + season (automatic, editable)
  -> Optional today-only comfort: Close / Comfortable / Relaxed
  -> Optional required/preferred color + affected piece/palette
  -> Optional starting point: “Use my brown leather jacket”
  -> Use Only My Wardrobe / Suggestions; new-piece ideas off until explicit choice
  -> STYLE ME

STYLIST
  -> Safe / Simple
  -> Stylish / Elevated — owned
  -> Another Elevated owned look, or explicit new-piece direction
  -> Verified limited selected source: labeled one-/two-look partial result
  -> Missing in this suitcase: refine here or explicitly switch to Main Closet

OUTFIT CARD
  -> Tap pants / top / jacket / shoes / accessories
  -> Swap real item / filter by color | Already Own | explicit optional Find One
  -> Outfit updates
  -> Earlier personal preview remains history; Update Preview explicit by default

CORE SAVE + FEEDBACK
  -> Save outfit
  -> Reopen/edit, Favorite, add to named private collections or tag occasion
  -> Search delivered previews and their garment/outfit metadata
  -> Learn owned items + preferences
  -> Continue styling her existing wardrobe

OPTIONAL MISSING-PIECE SEARCH — only when explicitly requested
  -> Find One uses saved fit preferences + confirmed shopping destination
  -> Sanitized public garment intent -> actual bounded web search and cited links
  -> Tool-free fit/style ranking -> sourced details, clear unknowns, a few options
  -> If unavailable: saved/manual alternatives or explicitly unverified browser search
  -> View at Store uses a permitted Safari web sheet or external store/browser
  -> Return restores the same outfit/product; optional Bought it? Add to closet
  -> User taps “I ordered this” / “I bought this” (explicit confirmation)
  -> Review actual size/color, source link, optional details and arrival status
  -> Add Camera/Photos image or use app-owned placeholder
  -> Save PurchasedConfirmed locally; not eligible for today until arrival is confirmed
  -> Optional local reminder: Mark arrived / Add photo / Add description / Later / Dismiss
  -> Add to This Outfit / This Suitcase only through a separate explicit action

OPT-IN ON ME / GENERATE LOOK — only if separately enabled after its gates
  -> Select optional reference photo + usable garment photos
  -> Review image permission and allowance/credit treatment
  -> Explicit Style Me may request previews for target three validated directions
  -> Keep every valid delivered preview and its exact version, then review/Favorite
  -> Search/revisit even unfavorited earlier alternatives; explicit scoped deletion

OPTIONAL ASK ANOTHER STYLIST — local export, no app AI call
  -> Preview permitted garment collage + editable prompt
  -> Explicit share/save/copy; receiving service's terms apply
```

### 5.2 Example

Input: “First day at a new job; office-like; not too flashy but cute. I have navy blue dress pants.”

| Lane | Example recommendation |
| --- | --- |
| Safe / Simple — owned | Blue trousers + navy cardigan + a suitable owned neutral top/shoes. |
| Stylish / Elevated — owned | Blue trousers + an owned light-pink blouse and suitable owned shoes, if those actual eligible records exist. |
| Another Elevated owned look / explicit new-piece idea | A distinct owned pairing when shopping is off; when explicitly requested, a clearly unowned matching piece with Find One and Save for Later. |

The examples describe ideas, not proof Lily owns each item. An interview request excludes inappropriate jeans/leggings unless she changes the occasion constraints. Tapping a garment shows closest supported real style/color/garment/shoe matches; she can choose, refine or manually replace it. An owned-only color change selects another eligible stored item, never repaints the current record. Other IDs/layouts stay fixed, and an existing personal image is labeled as its earlier version until explicit Update Preview.

## 6. Functional Requirements

| ID | Capability | Requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FR-01 | Flexible personal fit profile | Prioritize Lily's validated experience while storing editable optional height, inseam, sleeve length, waist and bust with units, measurement basis/source/update state, category/brand/sizing-system context, fit/style/color preferences and budget. Supplied preferred garment lengths remain distinct from body measurements. Lily confirms 4'11" height but has no confirmed inseam; earlier owner-supplied 25/26-inch context is unverified and excluded from sizing rules. Reuse confirmed well-fitting garments. Weight is optional context, never a sizing rule or prerequisite. No height/weight/size/gender eligibility cutoff or extra mandatory onboarding. Proportions-based petite guidance does not require a retailer Petite label; broader coverage requires separate validation. See Sections 10.1/10.2. |
| FR-02 | Affirmation | Display a brief positive affirmation on app open. Use a curated local library in V1 to avoid unnecessary AI calls. |
| FR-03 | Occasion selector | Provide quick chips for Office, Date Night, Brunch, Concert, Casual, Event, and Other. Additional occasions may be added without app redesign. |
| FR-04 | Weather + season | Automatically determine local weather and season. Allow override for future travel or planning. |
| FR-05 | Today's styling context | Provide an optional starting-item/free-text field plus lightweight request-scoped clothing comfort and required/preferred color controls. Default neutral comfort choices are Close / Comfortable / Relaxed; a required color identifies the relevant piece or palette. Do not change permanent measurements/preferences or create symptom/cycle history from a temporary request. Explicitly choose whether to refine this request or update a usual preference. |
| FR-06 | Useful outfit directions | Normal success targets three distinct directions: Safe/Simple owned, Stylish/Elevated owned, and another Elevated owned direction. Only an explicit new-piece choice permits an Elevated direction with a clearly unowned candidate. Zero-inventory Suggestions within Main Closet may show labeled hypothetical ideas; suitcase styling remains strict. PartialWardrobe returns one or two complete valid owned looks only when complete-current-scope evidence verifies limited alternatives; no valid look produces verified InsufficientWardrobe. Model/shortlist failure remains GenerationNotCompleted and cannot claim global wardrobe insufficiency. |
| FR-07 | Intentional coordinated color discovery | Respect hard color requirements and editable preferences while offering coherent pairings beyond habitual safe palettes. Lily favors chic, elegant, cohesive and less trend-driven looks; feminine/sporty depends on occasion. Blue/light pink and olive/brick are positive examples, not compulsory recipes. Explain relevant pairings briefly; explicit monochrome remains supported and strong red/green is a preference example rather than a blanket ban. |
| FR-08 | Visual outfit directions | Represent selected garments with accurate user photos or honestly labeled representative assets. Opt-in On Me targets personal previews of the validated directions only after Section 9.2 gates; boards remain the independent local/free fallback. Garment thumbnails/chips tied to canonical IDs support editing alongside the image. Unsupported pieces and approximate details stay explicit; no provider or physical-fit accuracy is assumed. |
| FR-09 | Direct real-item swaps | Every major piece is selectable. A committed one-item/color swap selects an eligible actual stored item in owned-only mode and preserves all other piece IDs/layout values. Offer closest supported matches and user refinement; no silent garment recoloring, ownership fabrication or forced choice. Retain earlier delivered previews with their original revisions; Update Preview is explicit by default and opening/scrolling the picker dispatches no image job. |
| FR-10 | Already Own | Allow the user to mark a suggested item as already owned with one tap. A photo and metadata are optional, not required. |
| FR-11 | Reviewed wardrobe learning | Persist confirmed owned items, colors/categories, real fit feedback, reversible preferences and saved outfits/collections. A real outfit-photo intake saves its source first, lets the user link existing items or review proposed Existing / Add owned / Ignore choices, and never infers ownership, exact size, brand or fabric. Saving a hypothetical outfit or a preview is not ownership or actual wear. Automatic multi-garment extraction remains a separately validated optional aid. |
| FR-12 | Find One | On explicit request, use real bounded AI web search with sanitized public garment intent, source-bearing retailer links and tool-free fit-first ranking. Prefer selected retailers including Amazon/TikTok Shop/Target/Walmart/H&M while prioritizing supported size/fit evidence and excluding known conflicts. Separate critical unknowns from supported matching guidance; preserve style/budget/destination hard criteria. No single-region restriction, catalog/fit-vendor prerequisite, affiliate monetization or retailer-account connection in V1. Manual browser search is explicitly unverified. Enable only after Section 13/RV-28/29 evidence. |
| FR-13 | Saved looks, Favorites and private collections | Save completed looks, optionally Favorite them, associate them with multiple named private collections such as Work/Gym/Going Out, and add editable occasion/intended-date tags. Creating/renaming/removing collections changes organization, not outfit existence. Delivered preview history is retained even when unfavorited or rejected; search/revisit uses linked metadata under Section 7.5. All relationships support local editing, sync/export/restore and scoped deletion. |
| FR-14 | Ask Stylist | Provide a lightweight secondary chat/action surface for questions such as “would white sneakers work?” without making chat the main UI. |
| FR-15 | Specific optional fit feedback | After an actual purchase/try-on/wear, optionally record “fits well,” “too long,” “too tight,” “too loose,” or “returned,” with optional affected area such as waist, seat, thigh, jacket length or sleeve. A return need not be a fit problem. Keep source/category context; a generated photo does not establish real fit. |
| FR-16 | Planning ahead | When a real forecast is unavailable, use labeled season/user-selected conditions. When forecast data arrives, update the weather context and offer explicit Restyle; do not silently change saved selections, regenerate images or dispatch paid styling. |
| FR-17 | Purchase confirmation | Durably preserve outfit/product context before opening a retailer. On return or from a saved reference, offer a nonblocking Ordered or bought it? Add to closet action. Only explicit confirmation changes ownership; browser closure, a click, or a receipt-looking page is not proof of purchase. Record actual size/color and independent arrival state; not-arrived/unknown PurchasedConfirmed remains ineligible until arrival is confirmed, even through availability overrides or laundry. |
| FR-18 | Add bought item | Explicit I ordered this / I bought this confirmation reviews actual purchased size/color and optional brand/date/source link, then durably creates/updates the intended canonical record without duplicates. Recommended size is not automatically purchased size. Not-arrived items remain ineligible for today; arrival does not require a photo. Support Camera, selected Photos/screenshots, explicit Paste Image/Link or text-only save with editable unknowns and optional completion reminders under Section 13.7. |
| FR-19 | Owned-item and real-outfit photos | Offer Camera/selected Photos for garment photos and user-created outfit-photo intake. Preserve original sources before optional processing; link/review actual items without mandatory extraction. Without a garment photo use an app-owned representative or text placeholder with known category/color preserved and unknown details labeled. Closest appearance is the quality target, not exact reconstruction of an unseen garment. Lasting retailer imagery requires explicit source/use rights. |
| FR-20 | Outfit boards and retained preview history | Show garments in a browsable closet and assemble saved/draft boards using user photos, labeled representatives or licensed assets. Distinguish owned/wishlist/inspiration/not-arrived items. Keep every valid delivered On Me preview, including earlier alternatives and rejected/unfavorited results, with exact outfit/item/image/reference revisions until reviewed deletion or applicable retained-content permission withdrawal. Reference/photo replacement preserves historical previews and relationships; preview/revert and Search work through linked records. Provider URL/cache/undo expiry is not local preview-history expiry. Local export follows Section 13.5. |
| FR-21 | Export / import backup | Export a complete, versioned archive of user-created data and garment photos through Files, including iCloud Drive. Import with preview, validation, and explicit merge or replace choice. |
| FR-22 | iCloud recovery | With optional iCloud sync enabled, preserve records, retained unprocessed source photos, active derivatives, saved layouts, and meaningful drafts for recovery after reinstalling or replacing a device. Show pending uploads, errors, and last successful cloud acknowledgement; resolve concurrent edits without silent loss. |
| FR-23 | Monthly styling access | Use StoreKit 2 to purchase/restore monthly subscription access and show subscription management. Support verified complimentary access and the owner-sponsored policy in Section 14. If try-on/credit packs ship, use a separate verified consumable balance. Preserve closet and backup access after expiry. |
| FR-24 | Complimentary codes | Support Apple offer-code redemption for a non-consumable styling unlock, with verified entitlement restoration and no recurring billing for the unlock. |
| FR-25 | Adaptive iPad experience | Ship native iPhone and iPad layouts from V1, including comparative outfits, closet grid/detail, contextual swaps, and resizable-window support. See Section 7.2. |
| FR-26 | Feedback board | Let users browse approved ideas and development statuses, submit suggestions, and vote. Provide private issue reporting, moderation, report/block actions, and deletion. See Section 22. |
| FR-27 | Improvement analytics | Offer independent opt-in analytics with a strictly limited event schema, withdrawal, and retention controls. Local personalization remains separate. See Section 23. |
| FR-28 | Cloud AI consent | Before sending personal styling context to third-party AI, identify the recipient and data categories, obtain explicit permission, and enforce the permission on both client and server. See Section 15.4. |
| FR-29 | Usage and cost protection | Enforce cross-device allowances, request limits, atomic cost reservations, bounded retries, and global spending circuit breakers. Show understandable allowance/reset states. See Section 11.6. |
| FR-30 | Privacy and account controls | Provide data export/deletion, optional feedback-account deletion, consent withdrawal, accessible privacy/terms/support links, and accurate storage/deletion status. See Sections 15 and 24. |
| FR-31 | Durable drafts and undo | Autosave accepted edits locally, restore meaningful drafts after relaunch, provide bounded undo/redo, and acknowledge saves only after durable commit. Failed saves preserve the last saved version and recoverable draft. |
| FR-32 | Manual outfit editor | Create/edit saved looks, choose stored pieces, change layout, save in place, or save a copy without AI, payment, internet, or remaining AI allowance. |
| FR-33 | Resumable photo imports | Securely save an unprocessed source first, resume interrupted imports idempotently, and keep optional image processing separate with preview, skip, retry, and revert. |
| FR-34 | Wardrobe availability | Mark currently owned garments Available, Dirty, Unavailable, or Archived locally. Show status badges in search/pickers/saved looks and exclude ineligible items by default; a visibly confirmed per-request override permits only selected still-owned IDs. NoLongerOwned/Trash/deleted records cannot become eligible through availability or laundry actions. Wearing does not automatically mark an item Dirty. See Section 7.3. |
| FR-35 | Use only my wardrobe | Enforce strict current stored-item references and availability on both provider output and client application. Never substitute hypothetical pieces; explain insufficient inventory and require explicit user choice to change mode. |
| FR-36 | Upfront capacity and access | No artificial subscription-dependent closet/outfit count cap in V1. Disclose pricing, AI allowances, import bounds, storage/recovery requirements before setup/work. The 1,000-item/500-outfit test dataset is a minimum validation target, not a product limit. |
| FR-37 | Honest item states and tags | Distinguish inspiration, wishlisted, owned, and user-confirmed purchased items. Only owned/confirmed-purchased items count as inventory. AI-inferred tags are editable suggestions with provenance; unknown brand, size, fabric, and exact fit remain unknown. Generic imagery is labeled as a placeholder. |
| FR-38 | Archive and recoverable deletion | Offer Archive to retain garment history, or Move to Trash with a disclosed 30-day recovery window. Permanent deletion removes the item's personal content and associated image copies, including retained edit history, with an explicit scope preview. See Section 7.4. |
| FR-39 | Local search and lightweight feedback | Provide Section 7.5's local keyword/metadata search across closet items, saved looks, collections, wishlists and linked delivered-image history without mandatory tags or cloud image analysis. Direct editing comes before optional reversible too dressy / not my style / wrong weather / don't suggest again feedback. Recommendation/save history can reduce repetition; actual Worn/date logging stays quiet and optional, and frequently selected/saved is not verified wear. Rejecting a preview changes preference, not its history retention. |
| FR-40 | Migration and conflict safety | Prototype versioned migrations, file/record recovery, and offline multi-device conflicts before confirming the persistence stack. No destructive partial migration or silent conflict overwrite; retain a verified recovery path. See Section 11.7. |
| FR-41 | Ask another stylist | Optionally select a look or closet items, locally render a legible collage/contact sheet, and prepare an editable prompt without calling any AI API. Preview, Share, Save Image/File, Copy Image where supported, and Copy Prompt remain available offline and after styling expiry. Receiving-app support varies; save/upload + paste is the fallback. No automatic external answer import, ownership change, or fit guarantee. See Section 13.5. |
| FR-42 | Product and support website | Provide a compact companion website with a truthful product demonstration, pricing/free-core explanation, fit and AI limitations, FAQ, About, support/legal links, feedback and release notes. Use the grouped footer and staged content plan in Section 22.3; the website does not add primary app navigation or require an app account. |
| FR-43 | Versioned release notes | Publish read-only, dated notes for public app versions describing user-visible changes, addressed issues and known limitations. Link only approved public feedback; private tickets and personal content remain private. See Section 22.2. |
| FR-44 | Optional early-user emails | Offer independently opted-in welcome and check-in emails for interested early users, with reply, unsubscribe and deletion controls. Never reuse ticket, Apple identity, billing or analytics contacts to populate the list. Participation is unrelated to app access; the program is optional and can follow the core launch. See Sections 15.4 and 22.4. |
| FR-45 | Contextual help library | Provide short task-based help in the relevant app screens and on the website. Essential in-app help works offline and remains accessible without an account or styling entitlement. Content follows the actual shipped behavior and does not invoke AI or upload wardrobe data. See Section 22.3. |
| FR-46 | Adult public launch scope | Target adults 18+ and initial United States App Store distribution. Approve proportionate age eligibility for personal cloud services, truthful age-rating/EULA handling and applicable privacy processes before release. Age eligibility is separate from AI consent. See Section 24.2. |
| FR-47 | Regional expansion foundation | Reuse the same app with separate storefront/service configuration, localizable presentation, explicit units and raw size/currency contexts. Additional countries require their own launch evidence and Lily regression checks; no automatic worldwide enablement or promise that every expansion avoids an app update. See Section 11.8. |
| FR-48 | Today's context | Capture optional current-request clothing comfort, required/preferred color and scope, manual weather and occasion context. Distinguish it from permanent fit/profile values; no inferred body/cycle state, symptom history, saved sensitive keyword or automatic AI dispatch on edits. See Sections 7.5/12. |
| FR-49 | Named collections and Favorites | Create/rename/remove persistent collections inside Saved, add a look to multiple collections and manage Favorites separately. Removing a collection never deletes its outfits/previews. Manual organization, sync and export/restore remain available without styling access. |
| FR-50 | Keep every delivered preview | Automatically retain every technically valid delivered generated output, including alternatives and disliked looks, as a durable image plus versioned composition and useful source-based keywords. Idempotent delivery creates no duplicate entry. No hidden history count/aging purge; explicit user/privacy deletion still applies. Favorites do not control retention. |
| FR-51 | Unified offline search | Search garments, original/real outfit photos, manual/saved outfits, all retained previews and permitted saved product records with keywords, phrases, prefixes, reviewed synonyms and facets. Show cross-entity/source labels, matching provenance and historical/current availability honestly. No API/image upload/login/subscription required to index/query. Metadata search is not proven pixel-based similarity. See Sections 7.5/11.9. |
| FR-52 | Recoverable search index | Build/update a bounded derivative index from durable canonical records in the background; show pending/error status and recover without data loss. Manual keyword edits update source revisions. Migration, sync, restore and deletion rebuild/reconcile without deleted-content resurrection; index entries never determine ownership or entitlements. |
| FR-53 | Reviewed real-outfit intake | Save a user-created outfit photo and let the user link existing items or explicitly confirm new owned entries. Optional inferred garment/match suggestions require Existing / Add / Ignore / Correct review. Preserve originals, unknown facts and reversible associations; no automatic exact size/brand/fit/ownership inference. |
| FR-54 | Works with my closet | On explicit shopping interest, show a bounded set of distinct combinations using the proposed unowned piece and eligible known inventory. Tie examples/counts to actual records and label source/fit unknowns; no claimed number of real wears. Optional personal-image examples follow separate image permission/cost/history rules. |
| FR-55 | Meaningful price comparison | When permitted products exist, compare an affordable and an investment-price alternative with sourced fit/price/stock/quality facts and uncertainty. Never invent a missing tier or infer durability from price/brand. Save for Later persists without purchase/ownership; retailer opening is a product-page handoff. |
| FR-56 | Full development with mock services | Develop the actual iPhone/iPad screens, storage and workflows using fictional fixtures and independently replaceable simulated stylist/image services before choosing production providers. Simulate successful and failing asynchronous image generation with permitted prebuilt demonstration assets. No paid inference, personal upload or real purchase is needed to test those flows. Use the production-facing contracts; clearly identify simulations and retain the separate live quality/integration gates. See Section 11.10 and criterion 77. |
| FR-57 | Private structured styling history | Durably retain useful outfit/explanation records and explicitly allowed structured styling context locally, with optional private sync and portable restore. Preserve provenance and dependencies without retaining raw conversations, symptoms or sensitive-text hashes. History remains user-controlled personal data, distinct from the short-lived backend result cache. See Section 11.11. |
| FR-58 | Revalidate before reusing advice | Check local history before paid inference. Distinguish a fully matching result from similar earlier looks; recheck current inventory, availability, occasion/weather, preferences and exclusions before reuse and application. Label reused advice and offer Use this look / Browse similar / New ideas. Fresh ideas bypass result reuse without automatically generating images. An older valid look is not proof of a fresh whole-closet evaluation. |
| FR-59 | Reuse retained matching previews | Before new image admission, find a permitted, intact retained output matching the exact render inputs and compatible configuration. Showing/reusing it incurs no new image dispatch or customer units; reserve only missing directions. Old-version previews stay viewable with captured-version labels. Update Preview checks reuse first; explicit Regenerate creates a new idempotent action. See Sections 9.2/11.11/14.4. |
| FR-60 | Personal garment names and additive details | Let users save a plain-language name such as Cute pink shirt, add other names/descriptions later, and edit/remove/undo them without knowing clothing terminology. Store confirmed item-scoped aliases separately from category/color/fit facts. Preserve stable garment IDs, photos, outfit links and earlier permitted names unless intentionally edited/removed; naming-only edits never trigger inference or preview generation. See Section 7.6. |
| FR-61 | Close matches and confirmed search correction | Prefer exact names/keywords; when primary search finds none, offer bounded, separately labeled possible matches without silently relaxing explicit filters or hard constraints. After the user selects an item, optionally remember an editable reviewed clothing phrase for that item only. Opening a result does not teach an alias, and ambiguous names require selection. Manual search/correction stays local, free and available offline. |
| FR-62 | Reviewed naming assistance and stylist references | Suggest simple names from known metadata; optional garment-photo understanding proposes visible details for review through its own validated local or explicitly permissioned cloud-analysis route. Preserve unknown facts and manual entry when analysis fails/is declined. Resolve a garment name to current canonical IDs before styling; ambiguous/unavailable/deleted matches cannot cause invented ownership or silent substitution. Text-stylist and try-on permissions do not authorize photo understanding. |
| FR-63 | No longer own, without deleting history | Add an explicit NoLongerOwned state, labeled No longer own/No longer owned, distinct from temporary Archive and privacy deletion. Preserve stable IDs, photos, names and permitted prior outfits/previews; exclude from current owned styling/swaps/preflight/reuse regardless of availability or overrides. Returning it requires explicit I own this again or safe undo, with availability rechecked. See Section 7.4. |
| FR-64 | Visible dirty status and deliberate exceptions | Dirty owned garments remain searchable with a text/icon/accessibility badge and their ordinary exclusion explained. Offer Mark clean or an individually confirmed Use for this request exception; viewing/selecting alone changes neither status nor permission. Current status accompanies historical looks without changing captured pictures/facts. Explicit Available-only filters stay respected. |
| FR-65 | Individual and confirmed bulk laundry | Provide individual Mark clean, selected dirty items as the default laundry route, and optional Mark all dirty items clean with reviewed IDs/count and confirmation. Transition only currently owned, arrived, non-Trash Dirty records to Available; keep other availability/ownership unchanged. Commit durably with idempotency, applied/skipped counts, safe compound undo and sync/conflict recovery. No AI call, preview generation or service-unit debit. See Section 7.3. |
| FR-66 | Multiple named Suitcases | First-release requirement: create, rename and manage multiple named garment subsets using canonical garment IDs, with no duplicate item/photo records. Main Closet includes clothing across all suitcases; removing a membership or archiving/deleting a suitcase preserves garments, saved looks and permitted previews. Offline organization remains free. See Section 7.7. |
| FR-67 | Visible wardrobe source and Settings default | Allow Main Closet or one named suitcase as the visible browsing/styling source, with a configurable remembered view in Settings. Capture and enforce the active source for garment lookup, starting items, all ordinary suitcase outfit pieces and swaps; explain insufficient inventory within that source. A valid empty suitcase stays selected. A garment may belong to multiple suitcases. Remember the latest selected source across launches; Settings can change it. Stay within a suitcase and offer an explicit switch to Main Closet when needed; never silently introduce outside garments. See Section 7.7. |
| FR-68 | Suitcase recovery, shared status and scoped laundry | Save groups/memberships/default references durably; include them in private sync/export/migration with removal/conflict protection. Ownership and availability are canonical across views. Distinguish This suitcase from Entire closet in bulk-clean review, recheck reviewed membership/eligibility, and safely resolve invalid source references without widening in-flight requests or spending AI units. Validate under RV-27. |
| FR-69 | Measurement-aware stylist policy | Versioned provider-neutral policy and typed minimal context use confirmed measurements with units/basis/provenance, real fit feedback and Lily's preferences; unknowns remain unknown. Separate hard constraints from style ranking, preserve canonical IDs/one-piece swaps and provide useful brief reasons. Evaluate and roll back compatible backend prompt/model/rule changes; never substitute prompt compliance for deterministic validation or real fit evidence. See Section 8.5. |
| FR-70 | Genuine web discovery and source validation | Separate public search from private fit context; require actual approved search-tool evidence for every lead/claim, typed success/empty/failure outcomes, safe links, permissioned media, finite aggregate tool/token/cost bounds and stale-action fencing. No browsing/write/checkout tools on the personal ranking stage, fabricated URLs, unattended refresh or guaranteed fit/stock/delivery. See Section 13. |
| FR-71 | Optional purchase completion reminders | After explicit purchase confirmation, offer an editable/dismissible in-app arrival/photo/description reminder, with a user-chosen local notification only if authorized. Persist privately, restore without notification duplication, cancel stale reminders on completion/deletion and revalidate the target before action. Free/offline with no AI call, required photo, inferred arrival or automatic suitcase addition. See Section 13.7. |
| FR-72 | Fit-first shopping and preferred retailers | Size/fit is a non-compensatory shopping requirement: exclude proven critical fit conflicts, keep unknown critical fit in Needs fit confirmation, and rank supported guidance by product-specific evidence/suitability before retailer/style/price/popularity preferences. Amazon, TikTok Shop, Target, Walmart, H&M and other permitted retailers are configurable editable discovery preferences, never sizing authorities. Validate exact product/seller/variant/chart basis and preserve other explicit hard criteria. See Section 13.8, AC-102 and RV-28/29. |
| FR-73 | First-use quick tours and replay | Provide a short, optional coach-mark tour for every shipped user-facing screen, including detail, editor, styling, shopping, settings and help screens; responsive layouts share a logical tour ID. Show each tour once on first visit, with Home after onboarding; Skip, completion or activating a highlighted control records it as seen. Profile / Settings > App tours replays any guide from step 1, even when the default-on Show tips on new screens switch is off. Use the approved soft spotlight treatment, simple wording, accessible focus and controls, and adaptive positioning. Persist version/outcome and the switch as local preferences with normal preference export/private sync/deletion behavior. Tours require no network, AI call, image credit, account or purchase and preserve the live task. See Section 7.8 and AC-103. |

## 7. Screen-Level Requirements

| Screen | Required elements |
| --- | --- |
| Onboarding | Petite-focused intro; skippable profile/measurements; optional fit/style preferences and budget; pricing/AI limits and privacy explanation before substantial setup. Offer a skippable welcome guide; start Home's first-use tour when onboarding finishes, subject to the tips preference (Section 7.8). |
| Home / Style Me | Brief local affirmation; visible Main Closet / named Suitcase source selector; occasion; editable weather/season; optional starting item; today-only clothing comfort and required/preferred color; Main Closet Suggestions / Use Only My Wardrobe, or strict Use only this suitcase for a suitcase source; explicit new-piece exploration off by default and distinct from strict suitcase looks; clear Style Me and separately enabled On Me state. |
| Results | Target three visual directions or clearly labeled verified one-/two-look PartialWardrobe; Safe/Elevated/conditional new-piece labels; concise rationale; image-ready/pending/failed status without blocking valid boards; selectable canonical garment chips, previous-preview label and explicit Update Preview. |
| Item swap sheet | Selected category, closest valid actual alternatives and color/style filters; choose/refine/manual alternative; Already Own; explicit Find One; no automatic image job on opening, color scrolling or selection preview. Commit changes one piece only. |
| Wardrobe | Main Closet / named Suitcase source selector, + Add Suitcase and membership management; visual grid with offline search by personal names/aliases/category/color/brand/notes; separate current owned, inspiration, wishlist and no-longer-owned/history views; editable names/details/tags, visible availability, Dirty filter, individual/selected/confirmed-all cleaning, No longer own, Archive/Trash, source/processed photo preview, resumable import status, labeled placeholders, Add Item from Camera/Photos/Paste/text, and select-items collage/share. Add details and Remember this name follow Section 7.6; no complete closet or technical terminology required. |
| Saved | Saved outfits, separate Favorites, named private collections with multiple membership, optional occasion/date and quiet Worn action; delivered-preview history searchable under Section 7.5 even when unfavorited/rejected. Show exact older versions, current missing/unavailable badges, manual editing, durable drafts and explicit AI variation/Update Preview. |
| Find One | Fit-first actual options; editable preferred retailers/destination/budget/style/color/garment/shoe criteria. Initial preferred pool Amazon, TikTok Shop, Target, Walmart and H&M, with other sources allowed. Separate Supported sizing guidance / Needs fit confirmation / known-conflict reasons; show relevant dimensions/evidence/unknowns and closest style alternatives only after fit. Affordable/investment directions when supported; Works With My Closet; View at Store, Save for Later and explicit purchase/arrival. |
| Profile | Optional confirmed measurements and well-fitting references; inseam unknown until supplied; aesthetic/fit/rise/budget/retailer preferences and separate AI permissions. Optional On Me reference appears only when the image service is validated. Replace updates future inputs while keeping earlier previews; explicit Delete has a scoped review. No body-photo prerequisite. |
| Bought item review | Confirm actual size/color/ownership and independent arrival; editable permitted metadata; Camera/Photos/screenshot/explicit Paste Image/Link/text; source-photo preview and optional local cutout; photo later; durable local save. Optional arrival/photo/description completion reminders; separate reviewed Add to This Outfit / This Suitcase preserves other selections and canonical identity. |
| Ask another stylist | Selected items/look, readable numbered collage, editable locally assembled prompt, optional personal fields default off, media-rights preview, Share/Save/Copy controls, and manual upload/paste instructions if the destination does not accept both. |
| Feedback | Approved ideas, search/filter, status, vote, submit idea, My Submissions, report/block, and a separate private issue form. |
| Settings / Privacy & Data | Default wardrobe view: Main Closet or a named Suitcase; App tours replay list and default-on Show tips on new screens switch (Section 7.8); cloud AI recipients and permission, analytics toggle, iCloud status, export/import, Delete My Data, optional feedback account/deletion, support, terms, privacy, and subscription management. |
| Help / What changed | Contextual task instructions, offline essential help and read-only versioned release notes reachable from Settings; no extra primary navigation tab or forced walkthrough. Optional quick tours are skippable on every step and replayable from Profile / Settings > App tours. Optional email participation is a separate explicit choice. |
| Styling access / allowance | Localized subscription terms, trial eligibility, meaningful usage allowance/reset time, restore/redeem/manage actions, and service availability. |
| First-use quick tours | Two to four short steps on each shipped screen, highlighting one real control at a time. Next advances in place; Skip or an accessibility escape closes the tour; the last button closes it with a clear action label. Replay any screen from Profile / Settings > App tours. Respect seen versions, the automatic-tips preference, active tasks, Dynamic Type, VoiceOver, Reduce Motion and iPhone/iPad layouts. See Section 7.8. |

### 7.1 Visual theme and color palette

Design decision added October 3, 2026: Use a warm editorial theme that feels like a personal styling notebook. The interface should feel calm, polished, and approachable, with garment imagery as the main visual focus. Keep everyday actions familiar to iOS users.

Define colors as semantic asset-catalog tokens with light and dark appearances. The following values are the V1 design baseline.

| Token | Light appearance | Dark appearance | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Background | Ivory `#FAF7F2` | Warm charcoal `#1C191B` | Main screen background |
| Surface | White `#FFFFFF` | Soft charcoal `#292529` | Outfit cards, sheets, and input surfaces |
| Primary text | Ink `#29242A` | Ivory `#FAF7F2` | Headings, body text, and important labels |
| Secondary text | Taupe `#6E626A` | Pale taupe `#C5B8C0` | Supporting explanations and metadata |
| Primary action | Deep plum `#633B55` | Soft plum `#D9AFCB` | Style Me, selected controls, and key actions |
| On primary action | White `#FFFFFF` | Warm charcoal `#1C191B` | Text and icons on primary action fills |
| Accent surface | Dusty rose `#F0E0E5` | Muted plum `#432F3D` | Selected chip backgrounds and quiet emphasis |
| Success | Forest sage `#42634E` | Light sage `#AFCEB6` | Ownership confirmations and successful saves |
| Success surface | Pale sage `#E7EFE8` | Deep sage `#273B2D` | Already Own badges and success backgrounds |
| Divider | Warm gray `#DED5DA` | Muted gray `#51464F` | Decorative separators and card boundaries |
| Error | Deep red `#A12D39` | Soft red `#FFB3B8` | Errors, destructive actions, and validation text |

#### Typography and layout

- Use the system font through SwiftUI. Use its serif design for the app title and occasional editorial headings; use the default system design for controls, body text, and metadata.
- Use Dynamic Type text styles rather than fixed font sizes. Body text starts at the standard `.body` size, and layouts expand as text grows.
- Use an 8-point spacing rhythm, typically 16-point screen margins, 16-point card padding, and 16-point card corner radii. Occasion chips use capsule shapes.
- Place garment images on a consistent neutral surface with enough space to see their silhouette. Preserve the garment's actual colors; avoid decorative image filters.
- Use SF Symbols for navigation and common actions, with text labels for unfamiliar actions.
- Use restrained transitions for card changes and swaps, respect Reduce Motion, and provide a brief confirmation after saving or marking ownership.

#### Applying the theme

- Home emphasizes occasion selection, weather, the optional starting item, and one prominent plum Style Me button.
- Results use consistent card layouts with Safe / Simple, Stylish / Elevated owned, and clearly labeled further Elevated owned or explicitly requested new-piece direction. Color alone never distinguishes their meaning. Verified partial results do not show empty invented cards.
- Swapping a piece uses a native sheet with clear alternatives. Already Own uses sage styling, while Find One remains a secondary action.
- Wardrobe and Saved use quiet surfaces and consistent image proportions so items are easy to scan.
- Follow the system appearance preference for light and dark mode. Keep garment imagery accurate in both appearances.
- Validate final text/background combinations against a minimum 4.5:1 contrast for normal text and 3:1 for large text. Meaningful control outlines, icons, and focus indicators need 3:1 against adjacent colors; the decorative divider token must not serve as the sole control boundary. Provide at least 44-by-44-point touch targets and VoiceOver labels.

### 7.2 iPhone and iPad adaptive experience

iPad support is a V1 product requirement, not an enlarged iPhone screen or a later delivery phase. Preserve the warm editorial theme and native controls on both platforms. Layout decisions use available window width/height, size class, safe areas, and Dynamic Type rather than device-name checks or orientation alone.

| Available space | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Compact iPhone or narrow iPad window | Native tabs/stack navigation, readable outfit cards, category swaps in a sheet or pushed detail, and one clear primary action. All returned directions, up to three, remain accessible without squeezing their text. |
| Intermediate window | Use two panes where readable, or collapse into a stack. Reduce grid columns and move secondary inspection into a sheet. Preserve the selected lane, garment, and draft. |
| Wide iPad window | Sidebar navigation with Style Me, Wardrobe, Saved, and Feedback. Compare up to three returned outfit directions side by side when readable card widths fit. Wardrobe presents an adaptive grid with selected-item detail; selected-piece swaps use a contextual pane/inspector so the current outfit stays visible. |

- Comparison and editing modes may use different pane arrangements. Collapse the inspector, then the sidebar, as space narrows; keep returned outfit cards readable rather than retaining a fixed column count. Bound card and reading widths on large displays while using remaining space for useful content.
- Support portrait and landscape, system-supported multitasking and resizable windows, software/hardware keyboards, pointer input, and safe areas. Do not require full-screen iPad use. Multiple app windows are optional in V1; if enabled, coordinate drafts and storage safely across them.
- Resizing, rotation, keyboard appearance, or navigation between panes must preserve the current request, unsaved form/feedback text, selection, saved/generated result, and useful scroll context. These UI changes must not trigger another AI request.
- Offer contextual keyboard commands such as Search, Add Item, Save, and Escape-to-dismiss where meaningful. Support Full Keyboard Access, visible focus, and native pointer behavior; touch remains sufficient for every action.
- On iPad, allow dragging an owned garment into the starting-piece control and dropping a user image or product link into item review. Validate category/source, preview the change, and retain explicit ownership/save confirmation. Provide tap, Photos, and paste equivalents; drag-and-drop must not be the only route.
- Reflow text through the largest accessibility Dynamic Type sizes; reduce columns or stack content before clipping labels. VoiceOver announces garment descriptions, lane labels, selection, pending/error states, and available actions. Respect Reduce Motion and never identify lanes or ownership only through color.
- Load downsampled thumbnails lazily for closet grids. Keep image decoding, archive processing, and network work off the main UI path. Provide empty, loading, offline, sync-pending, error, and permission-denied states in each layout.

Design and validation references: [Apple layout guidance](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/layout), [split views](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/split-views), [keyboards](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/keyboards), [pointing devices](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/pointing-devices), and [drag and drop](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/drag-and-drop). Test requirements are in Sections 16 and 19.

### 7.3 Editing, draft recovery, and unavailable garments

- Keep a durable local draft separate from the committed outfit/profile/item. Accepted editor selection, swap, layout, and tag changes are committed to the draft before showing a successful save acknowledgement. Text drafts autosave within a target of one second and on field exit/navigation where possible; indicate pending/failed saving honestly. Immediate Already Own/availability controls instead atomically commit the canonical item state, so later styling reads the acknowledged change. Do not depend on receiving an app-termination callback to save work.
- Restore active meaningful drafts after relaunch or interruption, retaining selected pieces, layout, request text, and editor position where practical. Let the user continue or explicitly discard. Private feedback drafts remain private and must not auto-publish on relaunch.
- **Save** atomically updates the existing outfit ID/revision; **Save as Copy** creates a new ID. Autosaving a draft must not silently overwrite the previously committed look. Explicit Discard returns to that committed state. Failed writes retain the old committed version and any safely staged draft; low-storage errors never claim success.
- Provide undo/redo for routine item/outfit/layout/photo edits. Keep a recoverable history of up to 20 actions, capped at 100 MB and seven days, clearly indicate when history expires, and clear personal history immediately on Delete My Data. Irreversible account/data deletion is separate from routine editor undo. Keep old photo assets while needed by this bounded history or live references.
- Manual creation, piece selection/replacement, collection/Favorite organization, layout changes, editing and saving work offline and remain free after expiry/quota exhaustion. AI suggestions and image refresh are distinct service actions. A manual change must not silently call AI, relabel an old explanation as newly validated, or overwrite an earlier delivered image. Mark a prior personal image as the earlier outfit revision and retain it for revisiting; Update Preview is explicit by default.
- Track an editor/closet revision on asynchronous work. A late AI, sync, or processing result cannot overwrite newer user edits; revalidate it and offer review/retry when its inputs are stale. Cancellable loading preserves the existing outfit/draft; cancellation stops UI application, but does not falsely claim a dispatched provider charge has disappeared.
- Retained delivered preview history is durable personal data, separate from the bounded undo stack and temporary result caches. Keep original image/outfit/reference lineage and searchable metadata for unfavorited or ordinarily rejected previews too. Replace Reference chooses a new version for future jobs and does not delete old previews. Explicit asset/item/data deletion or withdrawal of retained-content permission follows its reviewed scope and late-result fencing; do not promise retention of content the user has explicitly asked to purge.
- Availability is separate from ownership. Dirty/Unavailable/Archived garments remain in the closet and exports, but are ineligible by default for new AI looks/swaps. Dirty becomes Available through deliberate Mark clean; other unavailability/archive reasons require their own explicit action, never a laundry sweep. Optional unavailable-until dates remain editable. NoLongerOwned retains historical content but never counts as currently owned, regardless of its last-known availability. Saved looks retain their composition with current-status badges instead of silently dropping/replacing the item. Deleted records show a missing-piece placeholder and an intentional manual replacement action.

The default eligibility rule above permits an explicit exception: show the status and let the user allow individually selected Dirty/Unavailable/Archived item IDs that are still Owned/PurchasedConfirmed for this request only. Include those override IDs in the validated request, preserve their stored status, and recheck them on response application. An override cannot make inspiration/wishlist, NoLongerOwned, not-arrived PurchasedConfirmed, trashed, or permanently deleted items owned or eligible. Arrival is an independent user-confirmed field; changing availability or an unavailable-until date cannot imply delivery. Selecting a dirty item does not silently clean it or grant this exception. Marking an outfit Worn does not change laundry status unless the user separately confirms it. Keep text search and Saved within easy reach; no optional tag is required to find or edit an item. Authentication refresh for AI, billing, or feedback must not block opening the local store or using free core actions.

#### Laundry controls and bulk cleaning

Decision confirmed October 3, 2026: make laundry state visible and easy to update without treating a completed load as proof every garment was washed. Show a Dirty filter and text/icon badges in Closet, search, possible matches and starting-piece/swap pickers. Dirty remains searchable unless an explicit Available-only filter excludes it; never remove that filter silently. For an arrived still-owned dirty item, offer Mark clean and separately Use for this request. Use Once preserves Dirty. Both ordinary status review and historical outfits show honest current badges, without modifying photos or layout.

- **One item:** an explicit Mark clean button changes only that currently owned, arrived, non-Trash Dirty record to Available, acknowledges durable saving and offers Undo. No second modal is required for this reversible single-item action; a tap on its photo/result is not Mark clean.
- **One laundry load:** default to selecting the dirty items she actually washed, with no automatic all-selected state. Review the selected thumbnails/count, then Confirm Mark these N items clean. Cancel makes no changes. A selection does not persist a wash calendar or imply wear.
- **Full sweep:** offer Mark all dirty items clean — Entire closet as a separate shortcut, and Mark dirty items in this suitcase clean when viewing a suitcase. Gather the complete applicable canonical set of currently owned, arrived, non-Trash Dirty items, deduplicated by garment ID rather than limited to a filtered search page. Show Entire closet or This suitcase, the affected count and an optional review list, then require confirmation. A suitcase action rechecks each reviewed membership revision/removal generation and container archive/deletion state at commit; newly added members are not included, and removed members are skipped. All means those reviewed IDs; garments newly marked Dirty afterward are not silently included. Unavailable, Archived, NoLongerOwned, not-arrived PurchasedConfirmed, inspiration/wishlist and Trash/deleted records are excluded. Nothing is unarchived, returned from Trash, marked arrived or restored to ownership. If none qualify, show Nothing to mark clean. Shared garments show the same clean/dirty status in every view.
- **Durability and races:** record a stable local action ID and each target's expected ownership/availability revisions. At commit recheck those fields, deletion state and, for a suitcase load, the reviewed membership revision/removal generation and valid container state; atomically commit only still-matching targets, report cleaned/skipped counts, and never claim a full sweep succeeded when records changed. A newer Dirty event—even if the value is Dirty again—cannot be cleared by an older confirmed load. Repeated delivery/relaunch applies an action once. Low-storage/write failure preserves the prior committed state and truthful retry. Compound Undo follows the existing bounded recovery policy and cannot overwrite subsequent ownership/status edits; review conflicts rather than force old values.
- **Sync and service independence:** statuses/ownership and meaningful bounded recovery state sync/export with the closet. Show Saved locally separately from pending cloud changes. Competing cross-device status edits require the Section 11.7 conflict policy; uncertain eligibility stays excluded until reviewed. Laundry/name/ownership changes do not change appearance-input identity, purge history, dispatch AI, regenerate pictures, debit service units or automatically start shopping. Revalidate in-flight/reused outfit advice against the updated canonical states before applying it.

### 7.4 Archive, Trash, and permanent deletion

- **No longer own** records that she no longer possesses the garment, for example after donating/selling/discarding it. Set NoLongerOwned and remove it from the current owned view and all current owned eligibility, while retaining its original photos, names, permitted outfit/preview history and prior ownership snapshots. Show No longer owned on search/history results; availability overrides, cleaning, Unarchive and routine imports cannot overturn a known NoLongerOwned state. A deliberate I own this again or safe undo is required; preserve/recheck prior Dirty/Unavailable/Archived status instead of silently marking it Available. A different replacement garment is a separate item unless she explicitly confirms identity. This action does not delete retained content; scoped permanent deletion remains separate.
- **Archive** intentionally retains a garment's ownership state, images and linked outfit history while excluding it from ordinary new recommendations. It is appropriate for a still-owned item she has put away. Unarchive removes only the archive restriction after current ownership/availability review; it cannot undo NoLongerOwned or claim a garment is clean. Archive and Delete have different labels and consequences.
- **Move to Trash** hides an item from active inventory and begins a visible 30-day recovery window, separate from the seven-day editor undo limit. Before committing, preview the covered garment content and dependent retained-preview consequences of the eventual purge; offer Archive when the user wants ongoing history. Restore preserves stable IDs/links. Display the recovery deadline and Delete Permanently now. Trash uses storage and syncs removal state. This reviewed user-directed garment-deletion lifecycle is distinct from a standalone preview/cache/undo expiry; retained previews have no independent expiry-based purge.
- Before permanent deletion, preview affected images and outfit references. Purge garment metadata, image versions/thumbnails, associated drafts, undo/history copies, and garment content embedded in saved pieces. Retain only a neutral missing-piece placeholder and the remaining outfit layout; do not preserve the deleted garment in an old collage or conditional generated try-on image. Rebuild/invalidate derived collages and purge affected opaque generated images without silently paying to regenerate them. A genuinely shared asset may remain for another undeleted item, which the preview must disclose.
- Invalidate associated local personal result caches and clear the principal's normalized backend result cache when cloud styling was used; retain only necessary financial settlement state. Fence late AI/image/import/sync writes against deletion so they cannot recreate purged content. Provider-held prior context follows the disclosed processor retention/deletion terms; do not claim immediate erasure beyond the app's controlled copies.
- Permanent deletion and the disclosed expiry of user-directed Trash generate content-free deletion markers to prevent stale devices or an old merge archive resurrecting the record. Apply pending covered purges when the app next runs; show pending remote deletion until acknowledged. Do not repurpose this item-deletion policy into cleanup of unfavorited/disliked/superseded preview history. Storage pressure offers user-controlled export/cleanup or pauses new work, rather than silently purge retained previews; earlier permanent Trash purge requires explicit action. Delete My Data bypasses the recovery window and clears covered Trash/history immediately.
- New exports identify included Trash/expiry state and omit already purged content. Independently saved older archives cannot be erased by the app: disclose this and require an explicit reviewed recovery action if the user deliberately restores a deleted record from one. Routine merge/sync must honor deletion markers.

### 7.5 Unified search and retained-preview history

Garment browsing/search follows the visible Main Closet or suitcase source, with deliberate source switching; this does not limit access to Saved history. Add to suitcase is a separate, visibly labeled membership-management picker searching Main Closet under Section 7.7, not a styling-source switch. Saved can search all retained looks/previews and explicitly filter captured source when wanted. That filter uses captured provenance, not today's membership, so removing a garment from a suitcase does not hide its earlier looks. A historical match outside the working draft's source remains labeled and cannot silently become a current styling selection.

First-release search covers the user's local garments, original/source images, real outfit photos, saved/manual outfits, all retained generated previews and permitted saved shopping references. Search is a core local feature: no AI API, image upload, app-account login or active styling subscription is required to query or index these records. An image result is found from its reviewed keywords and linked outfit/item metadata; this is not a claim that the initial release understands untagged image pixels.

- Put one accessible Search entry within Closet and Saved, opening the same search surface with an editable scope. Keep the primary Style Me / Closet / Saved navigation. Within Saved, offer All Looks, Preview History, Favorites and user-created collections such as Work, Gym and Going Out; collection filters are not unlimited top-level navigation tabs.
- Support keyword and quoted-phrase queries, last-word prefixes, a bounded reviewed clothing synonym vocabulary (for example pants/trousers and tee/T-shirt), and filters for result type, category, color/shade, style, occasion, collection, favorite status, ownership, availability and image source. Preserve precise shades: expanded matches for related shades never rewrite a garment's confirmed color. Facets combine predictably; show active filters, remove one, and Clear All. Do not require users to finish tagging before searching.
- Search personal item names/confirmed aliases, title/descriptors, category and confirmed color/shade, reviewed style/occasion keywords, brand, editable garment/outfit notes, collection names, favorites, linked piece metadata and permitted provider/source labels. Primary results match all normalized ordinary content terms; an explicit quoted phrase matches an actual indexed field rather than an invented concatenation of unrelated pieces. If primary results are empty, Section 7.6 permits a separate labeled possible-match tier. Show which garment, annotation, note or linked snapshot supplied a match. Rank exact title/alias/phrase and confirmed-keyword matches ahead of synonym/prefix or indirect linked matches; ranking is deterministic and does not learn from undisclosed tracking.
