Current abuse monitoring permits flagged prompt logging for up to 90 days for applicable Cloud-terms customers; this is not evidence that every try-on image is routinely stored for that period. Confirm the actual image route/account terms, safety handling, input/output deletion and supported lifecycle before personal-photo testing. Consumer Shopping privacy/catalog features are separate. [Governance](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/resources/zero-data-retention), [abuse monitoring](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/abuse-monitoring).
- **FASHN direct:** documented base64 handling reduces temporary media retention; output URLs can expire quickly, and request records have separate retention. Do not put identifying content in prompt/URL metadata. Confirm the actual API account terms and successful/failed billing before selection. [Retention](https://docs.fashn.ai/api-overview/data-retention-privacy), [billing](https://docs.fashn.ai/api-overview/api-fundamentals).
- **fal-hosted models:** default CDN files can be read by anyone with their URLs. Configure and test input upload and output access/lifecycle separately; a payload-retention switch is not media deletion. Partner-model terms also matter. [File controls](https://fal.ai/docs/documentation/model-apis/file-access-controls), [payload retention](https://fal.ai/docs/documentation/model-apis/inference/payloads).
- **BytePlus direct:** current processing documentation describes longer safety-filter retention, including up to 180 days; an expired output link is not complete deletion. Review applicable terms/region before selection. [Data processing](https://docs.byteplus.com/zh-TW/docs/ModelArk/BytePlus_ModelArk_Data_Processing), [service terms](https://docs.byteplus.com/en/docs/legal/docs-service-specific-terms).
- **Higgsfield API:** its current terms allow training use by default unless an opt-out is processed, and metadata retention is separate. That must be resolved before any personal-photo pilot; a consumer subscription is not a privacy or API-rate contract. [API terms](https://open.higgsfield.ai/terms-of-service).

As additional storage scenarios, 50 retained generated images averaging 1–5 MB add roughly 50–250 MB; nine daily outputs over 365 days produce 3,285 retained images and roughly 3.3–16.4 GB at that illustrative average. Sources, captured metadata, thumbnails, reference versions, staging and independent sync/export copies add more. These are decimal-MB assumptions to measure, additional to Section 15.3's garment-photo estimates. Because retention is not limited to Favorites, budget history growth explicitly and offer user-controlled cleanup/export without silent expiry.

#### 9.2.5 Conditional TO pilot/release checks

All checks are **Not run**. They apply before try-on is enabled, including a limited beta; original wardrobe recovery/privacy/payment controls remain mandatory.

| Gate | Required evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TO-01 Inputs and coverage | Verify actual usable garment/shoe photos, supported categories/item count/poses and one to three distinct directions, including PartialWardrobe. Test single garment, separate top/bottom, cardigan/layer and closest shoe-style cases; provider candidate variants are not distinct outfits. Unsupported pieces stay selectable/board-visible and honestly labeled on the preview. No advertised category from an untested collage/pipeline. | Not run |
| TO-02 Fidelity | Prioritize Lily's consented reference/proportions and actual garment/shoe style, colors, patterns, logos and silhouettes; test blue/light-pink, olive/brick, trousers/jacket length and repeated swaps. Distinguish canonical ID preservation from pixels. Confirm representative unknowns and mismatch labels; no exact unphotographed-item/physical-fit promise. Document useful-look rate and unsupported cases, not only polished demos. Broader coverage requires its own evidence and Lily regressions. | Not run |
| TO-03 Personal-photo privacy | Named image-recipient permission, decline/withdrawal/recipient change, input/output ACL and expiry, log redaction, actual training/retention settings, source/output deletion and late-result fencing. No personal upload before these controls are verified. | Not run |
| TO-04 Durable history and search | Force-close during action/child upload, download and atomic history commit; relaunch resumes existing jobs without duplicate charges. Every technically valid delivered preview, including disliked/superseded, reopens/searches offline without API spend. Expiring URL, low disk/corrupt output and interrupted sync/export preserve prior assets; fresh-install restore matches hashes/snapshot/version links and rebuilds local search. | Not run |
| TO-05 Budget and partial readiness | Measure complete useful-look cost and p50/p95 latency for one/three-direction actions; exercise one ready/one pending/one failed, fewer available units, repeated taps, paid disliked/mismatched outputs, ambiguity/retries, multi-pass charges, deadline/cancel, unavailable ledger and kill switch. Per-child reservations/debits/release and parent admission prevent duplicate charges/allowance bypass. Pass criterion 56 for wallet recovery/non-expiry/after-subscription use and 57 for sponsored policy. No automatic overage or quality reroll; technical retries obey admitted attempt/deadline/cost bounds. | Not run |
| TO-06 Version, replacement and deletion | Switch working source/remembered preference or remove suitcase membership before new work and during admitted/dispatched work; Swap/edit/source-photo update while generating, ordinary Replace Reference, subscription expiry and provider retirement preserve retained assets/metadata/search with captured-version/source labels and target-context fencing. Scope provenance does not alter render identity or authorize a new job; already-dispatched permitted output remains Earlier history. Then explicitly Delete Preview, archive/Trash/permanently delete covered personal data, withdraw consent and Delete My Data during delayed completion; scope-sensitive purge removes covered images/descriptors/index content and prevents resurrection. Replacement must not act as implicit privacy deletion. | Not run |
| TO-07 Occasion learning | Ask for suitable occasions using metadata, edit/reverse tags, save/wear/like feedback, and relaunch/sync/export. Do not infer fit, wear, ownership or laundry status from a generated picture; no new image call for a metadata occasion question. | Not run |
| TO-08 Core independence | On iPhone 12/iPad and offline/expired subscription/image quota/provider outage, closet/manual editor/board and retained-preview search/view/organize/export remain usable. Try-on photo and named image permission stay optional. Navigation/manual edit/color scroll/reopen/restore trigger no generation; explicit Update Preview checks permitted exact reuse before admitting new work, and Regenerate is a fresh idempotent action. Shopping follows explicit user choice. | Not run |

Recommended next evaluation: start with Google's dedicated single-garment baseline and Lily's specific top/bottom/layer/shoe cases, then test a compatible multiple-reference or dedicated two-piece candidate if needed. Use consented material only after processor/privacy/budget approval; verify any Google successor or alternative's separate terms. Choose by closest actual source fidelity, proportions, waiting and cost per useful look. No provider selection, vendor contact, contract or paid test has been performed.

## 10. Petite-First Styling Layer

V1 validation is intentionally Lily-first and petite-focused. The underlying FitRulesEngine is profile-driven from day one, with an initial conditional petite/short-proportion rule set. Its applicability follows supplied dimensions, confirmed fit issues and preferences; short height does not automatically imply a short torso, narrow shoulders or a particular clothing size. Petite support should be visible in relevant fit metadata, options and explanations. Confirmed preferences and garment evidence govern her result.

- Evaluate petite/short proportions and actual garment dimensions where exposed. Proportions-based fit is the accepted default: a regular/cropped garment with better evidence may outrank a retailer Petite label. The label is neither required nor proof of fit; any later explicit category preference stays separate from her dimensions.
- Store height and measurements separately from weight; weight alone must never determine size.
- Track brand-specific fit feedback over time (for example: “regular sleeves run long” or “Petite S fits well”).
- Prefer garment proportions that are less likely to overwhelm shorter frames while respecting the user’s stated aesthetic.
- Never frame petite sizing as a beauty ranking; it is a fit/proportion constraint.
- Height and measurements are optional and editable. Offer inseam, rise, torso, sleeve, and fit preferences only when useful; missing values do not block styling. Petite does not describe a weight, and the user is not required to want to look taller. Explain guidance in terms of her stated fit and style goals, with no exact-fit guarantee.

Lily priorities confirmed by her interview: urgent work/interview help as well as nice occasions, casual outings and everyday polished looks; intentional coherent color variety; pants that are commonly too long; inconsistent waist/seat/thigh fit; and some jackets that are too long. Her rise preference is around the navel with modest variation, avoiding low and extremely high rise. She confirms 4'11" height, but inseam is unknown. Earlier owner-supplied 25/26-inch context is not a confirmed body measurement or preferred garment length and must not be used as a rule or silently averaged. Ask an optional useful measurement/reference question only when a specific pants comparison benefits, including hem/shoe context; never infer length from height. Sleeve length is editable but not a confirmed main complaint, and no blanket fabric exclusion is supplied. Separate firm requirements from ranking preferences and today's comfort from usual fit. On Me is her preferred opted-in visual experience under Section 9.2's gates; boards remain useful and free when imagery is unavailable, without claiming that a board-only test satisfies her full on-body preference.

### 10.1 Minimal setup for personalized fit

Personalized size and height/proportion guidance supports owned-clothes styling and the optional missing-piece finder; shopping does not define the primary experience. The preferred setup asks once for height, usual top and bottom sizes (including US/UK/EU or brand sizing), and preferred close/regular/relaxed fit. An optional reference is a brand, garment category, and size she confirms fits well; reuse existing confirmed wardrobe/fit feedback instead of asking again. No full closet, body photo, scan, app account, or retailer-account connection is required for ordinary styling. All inputs remain editable and skippable; skipped data reduces specificity rather than preventing basic styling. The optional try-on photo in Section 9.2 is a distinct image purpose and never creates precise measurements or validates physical fit.

Profile → Fit & Measurements offers optional inseam/preferred trouser length, sleeve length, waist and bust, with inch/centimeter display and plain measurement-basis help. Each value can be updated or cleared independently; no reset or new closet setup is required. Lily's inseam and other unsupplied numeric dimensions remain absent until she supplies a body measurement or known well-fitting garment reference. A short optional explanation of inseam can help without making measurement homework mandatory. Preserve her petite style goals and actual fit references with the current values; no weight-derived size or fixed waist/bust is assumed. Updating a field is local, not an AI call or automatic body-change tracking feature.

Ask a targeted follow-up only when it can improve the particular styling or fit question for owned garments or an optional purchase: inseam for trousers, for example, or desired crop-top length/coverage. Offer Skip and explain what remains unknown. Do not infer bust, waist, hips, torso, or sleeve dimensions from height and a size label. A brand's body-size chart and a garment's actual measurements are different evidence; any comparison must preserve that distinction and account for intended ease/stretch where known.

Keep style suitability, recommended size, and length/proportion evidence separate. A supported width/size recommendation must not imply verified crop length, rise, or sleeve fit. Use plain evidence such as “petite length offered,” “matches your confirmed size in this brand,” or “top length not provided.” Do not invent an accuracy percentage or promise perfect fit. Provider confidence is not a measured probability of success in this app; numeric confidence needs validation and an understandable meaning before display.

Retain optional Fits Well / Too Long / Too Tight / Too Loose / Returned feedback locally and include it in private sync/export. Optional affected areas such as waist, seat, thigh, jacket length or sleeve make the observation specific without requiring new measurements. Evaluate ease/stretch and conflicting fit evidence where known; a good waist result is not proof of seat/thigh fit. Store her navel-area rise preference descriptively rather than assume every brand's “mid-rise” means the same thing. A return is not automatically a fit problem; accept an optional reason. Do not read retailer purchase histories or transmit feedback to a fit vendor without separately disclosed permission.

### 10.2 Flexible foundation and protection of Lily's experience

- Store supplied measurements as optional typed values or explicitly supplied preferred lengths/ranges with units, measurement basis/method where known, source, last update and confirmation state. Keep body dimensions, preferred garment lengths, chart ranges and actual garment dimensions distinguishable. Unknown is absent, never zero. Preserve confirmed well-fitting garment references; height/weight/photo cannot fill missing dimensions.
- Increment the profile revision when a measurement/preference changes. New recommendations and fit assessments use the current revision; revalidate in-flight results and mark earlier fit assessments as based on older profile context, offering explicit reassessment where useful. Preserve saved outfit compositions and assessment provenance; a profile update must not alter stored garment dimensions, silently regenerate a look or remove her petite preferences. Values need not be retained as a separate historical body-measurement timeline.
- Store size labels with category, brand/sizing system, source chart context and supplied fit/cut qualifiers. Preserve the retailer's raw label; no universal S/M/L conversion or assumption that Petite, Plus, Short or Tall are interchangeable or exclusive dimensions.
- Keep user-selected clothing/catalog preferences separate from measurements and identity. Future men's/women's/mixed coverage must be explicitly supported; do not infer department or style from a photo or gender. Optional fields do not require a broader launch UI or mandatory new setup.
- FitRulesEngine evaluates application-owned versioned rules against the profile, garment evidence and user goals. Separate universal constraints from the conditional petite rule set; contradictory/missing evidence stays explicit. Preserve profile/rule revisions in requests and assessments, with compatible schema changes and rollback under Sections 11.3/11.7.
- Personalization is scoped to the user's profile. Other users' feedback cannot directly overwrite Lily's preferences or fit history. Shared model/rule improvements require evaluation; her saved scenario suite must pass before rollout. Do not weaken her fit constraints to improve average adoption or catalog coverage.
- Establish consented local/pseudonymous Lily fixtures for confirmed fit references, sleeve/inseam/rise/coverage preferences, different occasions/weather, independent swaps, skipped/changed measurements, reversible fit/style feedback and failures. Do not derive a clothing size from the 110–130 lb range or require weight tracking. Evaluate recommendations with her feedback; record issues and resolve them before declaring her experience validated. Keep actual personal content out of diagnostics/analytics.
- Expansion uses the same core app and per-profile rule contract, with its own garment/sizing/source/quality tests and all existing Lily regression checks. Broader fit or try-on support is not achieved merely by adding a profile option or changing a prompt. Conditional cloud services remain subject to their own consent/cost gates.

### 10.3 Observed Lily usability sessions

Decision approved October 3, 2026: make Lily-first validation a repeatable observation routine alongside the existing fit scenarios, reliability tests and regression suite.

- Once a usable prototype exists, run a brief session of about 15 minutes weekly during active iteration and after material changes to the core flow. Rotate real tasks: choose an outfit for an occasion, use a starting garment, swap one piece, mark Already Own, save/reopen a look, or recover interrupted work. Optional finder/try-on tasks apply only when those services are enabled.
- Start with her three interview tasks: last-minute work interview using blue pants and no shopping; blue-themed rainy event with current comfortable/relaxed clothing needs; and evaluating a useful new piece when she has time to shop. Include coordinated-color choices, pants/jacket length and rise preferences, unknown inseam and real garment references when supplied. Do not invent measurements, actual ownership of an example pink blouse, sleeve/fabric exclusions or a cut requirement. Observe whether boards and enabled On Me previews support her decision, recording their separate waiting time; photo evaluation follows TO gates and does not establish physical fit.
- Let Lily attempt the task without coaching. Record where she hesitates, makes a correction, needs help or abandons the task; ask what she expected after the attempt. If help is necessary, provide it and mark the completion as assisted. She may skip or end the session.
- For styling tasks, measure time from beginning the task to a look she says she would wear, together with steps/corrections, whether assistance was needed and whether she could save/reopen it. For add-item, editing, saving or recovery tasks, record completion time, errors and assistance instead. Record device/build and separate setup, interaction and provider waiting time. Record no acceptable look when appropriate; do not force a selection or turn a fast unusable result into success. This is a self-reported usefulness signal, separate from actual wear and physical-fit feedback; there is no unmeasured universal time target.
- Maintain a short decision log: task/scenario, build, observed friction, her explanation, proposed change, priority, retest build and result. Resolve the largest daily-use problems before expanding the experience; Section 23.2 data-loss incident priority still takes precedence. Add consented, minimized reproducible cases to the existing Lily regression suite.
- Use consented manual observation and private minimized notes, not automatic session replay, screen recording or wardrobe uploads. Notes should describe the interaction problem without copying measurements, wardrobe photos or raw prompts. Keep approved personal fit fixtures under Section 10.2's separate controls. This routine does not expand the Section 23 analytics schema or require analytics participation.

## 11. Technical Architecture

```text
iPhone / iPad SwiftUI app: adaptive styling, closet, saved, feedback
  |
  +--> Proposed SwiftData store: profile, closet, suitcases, outfits, collections/history
  |      +--> WardrobeScope: remembered source + canonical memberships/eligibility
  |      +--> Optional private CloudKit: records + sources/derivatives/layouts
  +--> WeatherKit / PhotosUI / Camera / Vision
  +--> Add Item / Name + details --> Durable local reviewed annotations
  |      +--> Optional GarmentUnderstandingProvider --> Review proposals
  +--> StylistEngine + FitRulesEngine + AIProviderRouter
  |      +--> Profile/evidence-driven conditional petite rule set
  |      +--> HistoryReuseService --> Revalidate private local advice first
  |      +--> Fresh/miss --> Apple Foundation Model when eligible
  |      +--> Fresh/miss --> Cloud when entitlement + recipient consent allow
  |             |
  |             v
  |           API Gateway --> Durable entitlement / consent / usage ledger
  |             |             Atomic quota + concurrency + cost reservations
  |             +--> Approved cloud model adapter --> Typed outfit validation
  |
  +--> Find One --> API Gateway --> Sanitized PublicShoppingIntent
  |      --> Approved bounded web search (no private profile/wardrobe context)
  |      --> Source/link validation --> Tool-free personal fit/style ranking
  |      <--> Local fit comparison; future catalog/fit adapters optional
  |      --> Evidence-bearing links --> Browser retailer checkout
  |      --> Confirm purchase/arrival --> Optional local completion reminder
  |
  +--> Local collage + prompt template --> Explicit native share/save/copy
  |      --> User-chosen external AI (no app/gateway AI dispatch)
  |
  +--> Local SearchService --> Rebuildable private keyword/facet index
  |      --> Canonical garment/outfit + retained preview snapshot resolution
  |
  +--> Opt-in On Me / Generate Look (disabled until prototype gates pass)
  |      --> Exact retained-output check --> Reuse intact matching pictures
  |      --> Admitted misses --> Private upload + job + approved TryOnProvider
  |      --> Durable retained preview history + exact outfit/item versions
  |
  +--> Optional feedback account --> Moderated public board / private tickets
  +--> Optional consented analytics --> Allowlisted ingestion / aggregates

Genuine AI web search is the first-release finder route pending Section 13 gates.
Catalog/fit adapters are later optional; no arbitrary server URL fetching.
Private closet content is separate from developer-held service state.
```

### 11.1 Client technology

- SwiftUI for all primary UI and navigation.
- SwiftData is the proposed local-first baseline for profile, wardrobe, outfits, collections, keywords, preview history and styling/fit feedback, subject to Section 11.7. Optional private iCloud sync has local-only fallback. SearchService maintains a separate rebuildable local index under Section 11.9 rather than treating an index as primary storage. Public feedback uses its own backend. No framework is assumed to supply all file/migration/conflict/restore guarantees.
- WeatherKit for weather context.
- PhotosUI / camera APIs for optional garment photos.
- SFSafariViewController for a retailer web sheet where permitted, platform URL opening for external browser/store-app handoff, and native ShareLink/UIActivityViewController plus explicit clipboard actions for local sharing. Validate destination-specific behavior; do not promise every app accepts an image and prompt together. A bounded share-extension inbox for incoming links/images is a separate implementation task with the same durable import rules.
- Vision for on-device foreground extraction or image processing where useful.
- Foundation Models framework through SystemLanguageModel when the device is eligible and Apple Intelligence is ready.
- Minimum deployment target must support Lily’s iPhone 12; exact iOS floor to be chosen after device testing.

### 11.2 Backend responsibilities

- Protect stylist, image and web-search credentials on the backend; never ship secret keys in the app bundle. Select/validate a genuine search adapter under Section 13; catalog/fit vendors are later optional integrations.
- Verify the relevant subscription, complimentary or purchased-image entitlement and enforce its approved public/sponsored policy plus technical/global controls. Lily's authorized sponsor policy does not inherit public daily/monthly service quotas; no client can bypass financial or permission admission.
- Normalize provider responses into the app’s typed schema.
- Validate every cloud response against the shared versioned schema and product rules before returning it; reject invalid results and use bounded retries or a configured fallback.
- Keep cloud model selection and supported inference settings in server configuration from day one; do not hardcode the cloud model ID in the iOS app.
- Cache safe repeatable results where appropriate (for example licensed public catalog metadata). Long-lived personal styling history and context-aware reuse stay local/private under Section 11.11; the gateway's encrypted principal-scoped completed-action cache still expires within 24 hours under Sections 11.6/15.4.
- Collect minimal operational telemetry; no wardrobe/photo upload unless required by a selected feature and disclosed to the user.
- Maintain a durable entitlement/usage ledger and shared atomic limits across all gateway instances. A stateless function's memory is not an enforceable quota store.
- Host the optional feedback account, moderated board, private support queue, and consented analytics ingestion separately from the user's private closet. Apply access controls, retention, and deletion to each service.
- Route explicit Find One through sanitized public search, source/link validation and a separate tool-free personal ranking request. Disclose/authorize each processor and reserve full tool/model costs before dispatch. Enforce bounded supported tools; no unrestricted agent, arbitrary MCP access or purchases. Local fit comparison stays on device where possible; the approved ranking processor receives only permissioned minimal fit context. No private profile/wardrobe context reaches the public search stage.

### 11.3 AI provider routing

At request time, the app checks Apple Foundation Model availability. Apple documents that SystemLanguageModel availability can be unavailable because the device is not eligible, Apple Intelligence is disabled, or the model is not ready. Eligible devices use the local provider; Lily’s iPhone 12 uses the cloud fallback. The app-facing schema remains identical either way.

#### Server-configurable cloud model selection

The backend selects the cloud provider, model ID, and supported reasoning settings through server configuration from the first release. The iOS app sends a StyleRequest and receives the shared typed response; it does not depend on a particular cloud model name. A compatible model upgrade or rollback must require only a backend configuration change, with no app rebuild or App Store update.

- Keep provider-specific prompts, API parameters, and response normalization in backend adapters. Switching providers may require a new or updated adapter, while preserving the app-facing contract.
- Validate the versioned complete/PartialWardrobe/failure contract, Safe/Elevated semantics, shopping permission, confirmed-color and starting-item constraints and canonical item references. Schema compliance alone does not establish styling quality. V2 response/UI changes require compatible app support; legacy clients must retain their supported contract until upgraded.
- Evaluate model changes on representative styling and swap scenarios before rollout, checking quality, constraint adherence, latency, and actual cost. Retain the previous configuration for rollback.
- Include Lily's Section 10.2 saved scenarios before any model, fit-rule or broader-coverage rollout; respect her confirmed fit/style constraints and preserve simpler daily interaction. Additional audiences cannot bypass this check.
- Support configured fallback routing for failures; introduce routing to a stronger model for difficult requests only when evaluation demonstrates a benefit and cost limits permit it.
- Treat the provider's legal recipient, data categories, retention terms, and approved model/cost bounds as part of versioned server configuration. A different recipient or material sharing change requires updated disclosure and permission before dispatch; a server-only model switch must never bypass consent.
- Preserve compatibility with released clients when evolving the schema. New fields or UI behavior that require client support may still need an iOS update.

### 11.4 Technology stack summary and open choices

The V1 application is a native iOS app written in Swift, built in Xcode. The following stack consolidates the architecture described above; it does not imply that an implementation already exists.

| Layer | Technology / decision | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Language and UI | Swift and SwiftUI, with SF Symbols and semantic asset-catalog colors | Defined |
| Local persistence | SwiftData baseline for profile, wardrobe, saved outfits, and feedback | Proposed; confirm after migration/conflict/recovery prototype |
| Optional private sync | Private iCloud database, using CloudKit-backed persistence; local use remains available | Defined; schema and sync behavior require validation |
| Weather | WeatherKit | Defined |
| Photos and image processing | PhotosUI, camera APIs, and Vision where useful | Defined |
| On-device AI | Apple Foundation Models through SystemLanguageModel, gated by device and runtime availability | Defined for eligible devices |
| Cloud AI | CloudLLMProvider behind the same typed stylist contract; required for Lily's iPhone 12 | Provider and model to be selected |
| Shopping and fit | Server-configurable bounded web-search adapter, typed source evidence/safe links, tool-free fit/style ranking and local fit comparison; later catalog/fit adapters optional | Accepted first-release approach; real source/coverage, privacy, costs and Lily usefulness require Section 13 and RV-28/29 evidence |
| Checkout and optional external AI handoff | Safari web sheet when permitted; external URL/app opening; local collage/prompt assembly and native share/save/copy | Specified; retailer exceptions and receiving-app compatibility require device tests; no external-AI API dependency |
| Opt-in personal preview | Server-configurable image provider and bounded parent/child jobs, private uploads, atomic reservations, durable searchable history linked to exact versions | Preferred Lily mode; provider/terms/garment-shoe-layer quality/allowance unselected; Section 9.2 TO evidence required |
| Unified private search | Local SearchService and rebuildable keyword/facet/relationship index; SQLite FTS5 is a prototype candidate | Required offline UX; implementation and relevance/performance/restore prototype under Section 11.9 |
| Client networking | URLSession and Codable for validated typed requests and responses | Defined |
| Backend | Serverless API gateway for cloud credentials, request validation, rate limits, and cost controls | Hosting platform, language, and authentication approach to be selected |
| Durable backend state | Transactional entitlement/usage and reservation ledger, consent records, optional feedback identities/posts/votes/reports, and moderation audit | Database/vendor TBD; atomic operations and separation from private closet required |
| Feedback identity | Optional Sign in with Apple for public submission/voting; browsing and core closet/purchases remain usable without this account | Defined; token verification, revocation, and deletion required |
| Product analytics | First-party allowlisted events, optional consent, bounded ingestion/retention, and aggregate dashboards | Defined; storage/operations implementation TBD |
| Testing | Unit tests for schemas, routing, learning, weather, and swaps; UI tests for the primary styling flow | Defined; test framework to be selected during implementation |
| Distribution and billing | Free App Store download; StoreKit 2 monthly subscription, non-consumable complimentary unlock via Apple offer codes, and conditional consumable image packs | One month free for eligible users; US$9.99/month is a new candidate, final price/allowances/product IDs TBD. Owner-sponsored usage is separate from public quotas. |

Choose the minimum iOS deployment target after verifying framework availability and testing on Lily's iPhone 12. Gate newer framework features behind availability checks. Private sync and the cloud gateway must not make an app-specific account mandatory for the core local experience.

### 11.5 Weather integration and cost notes

Use Apple WeatherKit for current conditions and forecasts that inform layers, fabric, footwear, and precipitation suitability. WeatherKit offers a native Swift API on iOS 16 and later, with forecasts extending up to 10 days. For dates beyond the forecast window, use season and user-entered conditions rather than presenting an unavailable forecast as known.

#### Included allowance and paid usage

Pricing checked October 3, 2026, in USD; recheck before launch.

- WeatherKit includes **500,000 API calls per month per Apple Developer Program membership**, shared across that membership's apps and users. This is not a per-user allowance.
- Apple Developer Program membership is **$99 per year** (or local equivalent) and also covers app distribution. Within the included quota, weather has no additional usage charge.
- Higher monthly quotas cost **$49.99 for 1 million calls** or **$99.99 for 2 million calls**. Unused calls do not roll over.
- The developer pays these costs; app users do not need their own weather API subscription.

Planning estimate: one weather request per session, three sessions per day, and a 30-day month.

| Daily active users | Estimated monthly calls | Within included quota? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1,000 | 90,000 | Yes |
| 5,000 | 450,000 | Yes |
| 10,000 | 900,000 | No; requires a higher quota |

These estimates exclude extra refreshes, retries, and requests for other locations or dates. Measure actual API usage during beta testing.

#### Request handling and user experience

- Reuse the session's weather snapshot for outfit generation, regenerations, and item swaps. Fetch again when the location or planned date changes, or the snapshot becomes stale; define freshness limits during implementation.
- Request only the weather data needed for styling and monitor the membership's monthly usage.
- Allow manual location and weather overrides when location access is declined or WeatherKit is unavailable. Label stale data and manual overrides clearly.
- Display the required Apple Weather attribution and legal link wherever weather data is shown, following Apple's current attribution requirements.

Sources: [Apple WeatherKit pricing, availability, and attribution](https://developer.apple.com/weatherkit/) and [Apple Developer Program membership](https://developer.apple.com/programs/).

### 11.6 Cloud service abuse and cost protection

These are V1 requirements. The gateway must protect developer-paid services for trial, paid, complimentary, purchased-credit, and owner-sponsored users alike. The following are proposed public launch defaults, not Apple/provider limits; validate them against real usage before publishing the allowance or enabling production billing. Controls live in versioned server configuration and cannot be overridden by client input. Lily's authenticated owner-sponsored policy may replace public daily/monthly service allowances and principal spending budget with separately approved values; it never bypasses input bounds, idempotency, technical throttles/concurrency, permission, or global financial admission. A public complimentary code alone does not authorize that exception.

#### Allowances and request bounds

| Control | Proposed starting setting |
| --- | --- |
| Successful full cloud generations/regenerations | 10 per styling principal per rolling 24 hours; one generation returns all three lanes |
| Successful cloud swaps + Ask Stylist | 40 combined per styling principal per rolling 24 hours |
| Rapid-request throttle | At most 5 AI actions/minute per principal, with additional installation/short-lived IP abuse limits |
| Concurrent expensive work | 1 admitted parent action per principal across devices. An enabled On Me set may contain at most 3 requested child preview jobs, processed under Section 9.2's bounded worker policy; initial default 1 active image child at a time. No additional parent admission or growing unattended queue while it runs. |
| Text-provider attempts | At most 2 total per text action, including retry, JSON repair, stronger-model escalation, and fallback; image children use Section 9.2.4's separately approved bounds |
| Provider input | At most 8,000 model tokens, including system rules and selected closet context; retrieve relevant records instead of sending the whole closet |
| Output including billed reasoning | At most 3,000 tokens for a full generation; 1,000 for swap/chat, enforced using the selected provider's supported charge-bounding controls |
| Request payload | At most 64 KiB, with free-text notes at most 2,000 characters; validate field sizes, collection counts, enums, and nesting |
| Idempotency | Retain action keys and permitted typed-context fingerprints for at least 24 hours; bind purpose, working source/mode, relevant context/version and transient allowed IDs to the action. Duplicate delivery reuses that action; the same key with a different typed context is rejected, never reused across scopes or charged as another request. Use opaque context revisions for edits, not surrogate hashes of sensitive prose; normalized-result cache retention remains at most 24 hours |
| Text-action deadline | Initial text-action deadline 30 seconds across all attempts; bound each attempt and any backoff within this deadline. Image actions use Section 9.2.4. |
| Internal beta inference budget | Public-policy initial guardrail US$1 per principal per rolling 30 days, including failures/retries; validate before launch. Owner-sponsored usage needs its own approved principal budget; set approved production values for each policy. |
| Global financial exposure | Explicit daily/monthly budgets per environment and provider are required before external testing; unset/invalid budgets disable expensive dispatch |

These token/payload/deadline defaults govern text styling. They do not authorize photo uploads or unlimited image generation. If Section 9.2 is enabled, its separate image route requires approved finite upload/pixel/input/output bounds, asynchronous job deadline and category allowance while still obeying shared aggregate principal/global reservations and concurrency controls. The public US$1 guardrail must not be silently exceeded by image work; revise/approve combined public budgets and the separate owner-sponsored budget before an image pilot. Purchased credits authorize customer usage, not a bypass of financial reservations; their approved cost ceiling must support the advertised redeemable service. Metadata/status calls keep normal bounded request behavior.

Public daily allowances must support the intended three-session day and reasonable experimentation. Disclose the shipped limits before trial/purchase and in Settings; never advertise unlimited AI. Do not silently reduce an existing paid period's promised allowance through remote configuration. Internal cost cutoffs must not routinely prevent advertised normal usage: if testing shows that they do, change pricing, model/caps, or approved budgets before release.

Count complete successful text actions against the daily allowance. Verified PartialWardrobe and failures do not consume a successful-full-generation unit, but attempts consume defensive throttles and dollar budgets; no unlimited free retry is permitted. Separately delivered image jobs follow the disclosed image-unit policy regardless of whether their parent text result was partial. Reserve successful-action slots atomically. Display category-specific remaining allowance/reset and distinguish throttle, exhaustion, expiry and shutdown; preserve personal work. No automatic charge or top-up. Eligible on-device text generation follows access/schema rules without cloud inference units; personal image work still uses its own approved service policy.

#### Identity and atomic financial admission

- Authenticate short-lived backend sessions and verify signed Apple transactions, app/product/environment, expiry, and revocation before paid work. Establish a durable pseudonymous styling principal from verified entitlement evidence, independent of an optional feedback account. Document the accountless purchase/restore authentication flow before launch; client IDs or a client-supplied transaction ID alone are insufficient.
- Aggregate usage across iPhone/iPad installations and restored entitlements. Reinstall, a new attestation key, a device switch, restoration, consent withdrawal, or deletion of closet/analytics/feedback-account data must not reset the associated allowance. Keep only the minimum legally permitted usage-window anti-abuse state, separate from deletable personal content and disclosed in the retention policy. Explain any supported Family Sharing and entitlement-merging behavior before enabling it; avoid simultaneous subscription/complimentary entitlements minting duplicate budgets.
- Supplement with App Attest on supported devices. Validate the app/environment, one-time server challenge, request binding, and replay counter. Provide a documented bounded recovery path for unsupported or temporarily unavailable attestation; it must not grant unrestricted API access. App Attest is an abuse signal, not proof of payment. Shared IP addresses alone must not permanently deny a legitimate subscriber.
- Before **every provider attempt**, estimate a conservative maximum cost from validated input, current approved prices, output/reasoning limits, and per-call charges. Atomically reserve that cost against principal, provider, and global daily/monthly budgets in a durable shared ledger. Include settled cost **plus outstanding reservations** when deciding admission. All gateway instances/regions must share the same enforceable state.
- Dispatch only after entitlement, consent, schema, rate, successful-action slot, concurrency, and financial reservations succeed. Settle reported actual usage afterward. Each second attempt needs another reservation. A fallback or new model never bypasses the ledger.
- An ambiguous timeout or disconnected client is not proof of zero cost or provider cancellation. Keep the conservative financial reservation until reconciliation establishes the outcome; a worker lease expiry must not erase it. Do not blindly retry an uncertain paid dispatch. Missing usage is not zero usage.
- If entitlement/consent verification, the ledger, pricing, or charge-bounding configuration is unavailable, fail closed for new expensive requests and preserve local functionality. No in-memory-only counter, client-only limiter, or read-then-increment race satisfies this requirement.
- Same action key with changed content is rejected. Duplicate delivery returns the existing status/result without another quota debit or dispatch. Encrypt any normalized personal completed-result cache, scope it to its principal, and expire it within 24 hours; raw prompt/response retention in logs is prohibited. Recheck current consent/deletion version before saving or delivering a delayed personal result; after withdrawal/deletion discard it and do not recreate the cache. Recheck recipient permission before each retry/fallback. Financial settlement still accounts for already dispatched work without retaining its personal content.

#### Operational defenses and other metered services

- Alert the operator at 50%, 75%, and 90% of financial budgets; stop new admission at the configured ceiling. Provide immediate global and per-provider kill switches, including the ability to disable costly features. Apply vendor-side hard limits where actually supported and distinguish them from alert-only dashboards.
- Bound execution time, input parsing, queue depth, response size, background jobs, and ingestion rates. Retry transient errors only with bounded backoff/jitter and the total attempt budget; do not retry invalid requests, authentication failures, quota/budget rejection, or permanent errors.
- Monitor hosting/database/storage/egress, product discovery, specialist fit, feedback, analytics, and WeatherKit costs separately from model tokens. Metered shopping calls use the same verified styling principal and shared reservation ledger, with their own disclosed allowance finalized after the Section 13 prototype. Use deployment/platform quotas and public-endpoint/WAF limits so unauthenticated floods cannot run unbounded functions or fill databases. Close submissions/ingestion temporarily during abuse; retain closet/support access where possible.
- Weather uses the unchanged-location/date session snapshot for at least 30 minutes, coalesces simultaneous lookups, and allows at most one automatic retry. Explicit new location/date requests and expired snapshots may refresh. Warn at 75%/90% of the membership quota and switch to clearly labeled cached/manual conditions before an exhausted allowance creates repeated failures. Any shared weather cache must respect Apple terms and avoid retaining precise personal locations.
- Base V1 garment photos remain local/private iCloud except the user's explicit local export/share in Section 13.5 or deliberately requested, independently validated cloud photo capabilities in Sections 8.4/9.2; the text-styling gateway rejects image uploads. Optional garment-understanding and try-on routes each stay disabled until their own named recipient/purpose/data permission, encoded-size/decoded-pixel bounds, metadata removal, deadline, retention, deletion and cost gates pass. Only those separately validated routes may accept their approved selected images; one route's permission cannot authorize the other, and unsolicited/unauthorized uploads are rejected. Local collage/template sharing itself invokes no model or paid fit/search service.
- Do not fetch arbitrary pasted retailer URLs on the backend in V1. Treat notes, garment descriptions, imported records, product links, and model output as untrusted data. They cannot change entitlements, budgets, provider/model selection, prompts' authority, credentials, or tool permissions. V1 AI has no arbitrary tool/code execution.
- A model rollout must pass quality, schema, consent compatibility, price/output-bound, latency, and budget checks. Keep an audited rollback. A more expensive model is not permitted to silently invalidate published allowances.
- These controls bound authorized dispatch and reduce exposure; exact invoices can still differ due to in-flight work, vendor billing/reporting, infrastructure charges, or compromise. Maintain emergency key rotation, kill-switch, reconciliation, and incident procedures.

Security references: [Apple App Attest server validation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/devicecheck/validating-apps-that-connect-to-your-server), [attestation availability](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/devicecheck/dcappattestservice/issupported), and [OWASP API resource-consumption guidance](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa4-unrestricted-resource-consumption/). Detailed verification obligations are in Sections 19 and 24.

### 11.7 Persistence prototype, migrations, and bounded waiting

Suitcase and membership edits use stable IDs, revisions and removal markers. Removing a membership, archiving/deleting its container, or deleting a garment cannot be undone by a stale sync/import or delayed job. Merge independent membership edits; retain competing container/default edits for review, without changing an open draft's scope. A saved-source reference not yet downloaded remains visibly unresolved with temporary Main Closet browsing access, without authorizing broader styling; confirmed deleted/archived references reset to Main Closet. Preserve valid old local scope data while receiving recovery records. Track unresolved references and incomplete membership/item relationships separately from valid empty; Section 7.7 readiness is required before any verified scope-wide absence/count. Test preference/membership conflicts and migration/export integrity alongside canonical garment recovery.

Before confirming SwiftData/CloudKit as the persistence implementation, build a recovery prototype with versioned schema fixtures, representative images, durable drafts, and two offline devices. Verify atomic record/file linking, original-inclusive export/restore, migration rollback, and recoverable conflict resolution. If the baseline cannot meet these gates, choose a different persistence implementation behind the same services before committing production user data.

- Migrations stage a versioned recovery snapshot, verify free space and integrity, and journal progress before changing the live store. An interrupted/failed migration leaves the old store recoverable; unsupported schemas open a recovery/export path instead of resetting the closet. Validate old-to-current fixture counts, IDs, image hashes, relationships, and restart at each boundary. A recovery snapshot is protected personal data and follows the deletion/retention rules; remove it after verified upgrade recovery within seven days.
- Merge nonconflicting edits; preserve competing revisions and show a conflict review when the same value/layout changed. Do not select a winner solely from a device clock. Delete/Trash/archive/NoLongerOwned versus stale edits has explicit precedence: removal from eligibility stays excluded until a reviewed restore, and permanent deletion markers prevent resurrection. NoLongerOwned cannot become owned through a stale availability/label update or old-archive merge; explicit I own this again must reference/review the current removal state. For competing clean/Dirty/Unavailable updates, keep uncertain eligibility excluded until reviewed rather than clearing newer restrictions. A delayed bulk-clean action carries its reviewed IDs and expected ownership/availability revisions, not a command to clean whatever is dirty on a receiving device. Test offline edits on both devices, reconnect in both orders, repeat delivery, and recover without duplicate records, lost status changes or silent overwrites.
- App-controlled interactive network operations have cancellation, a declared overall deadline (initial default 30 seconds; enabled image actions use Section 9.2.4's bounds), bounded attempts, and useful status/recovery choices by ten seconds. Preserve input and expose saved/manual alternatives. Background uploads/downloads use durable per-entry state and bounded worker attempts; a slow transfer may remain visibly Pending with pause/retry while local work continues. Cancellation never implies a dispatched charge was refunded.
- CloudKit synchronization and StoreKit pending purchases follow their system-managed lifecycle rather than a fabricated completion deadline. Keep their status explicit and free local actions responsive; do not blindly restart a pending purchase or force an OS background operation to claim success. Test authentication refresh/expiry, malformed responses, 429/5xx, and cancellation in each app-owned service.

### 11.8 United States launch and regional expansion foundation

Decision confirmed October 3, 2026: launch publicly in the United States for adults 18+, then consider other countries with similar fit needs after validation. Country expansion reuses the same client/service contracts and profile-driven fit foundation; do not encode Lily's height or a presumed national body type as a country rule.

- Keep App Store distribution, approved cloud-service/processor eligibility, UI language, measurement display, garment sizing system, StoreKit price/currency, weather/travel location and shopping delivery destination distinct. Changing one must not silently rewrite the others, confirmed profile values or saved looks.
- Use localizable strings and locale-aware date/number/unit presentation from day one. Preserve canonical measurements with explicit units/basis and original brand/category size labels; display conversion does not change the underlying fit evidence. Use actual localized StoreKit prices, never a country-based hardcoded subscription amount.
- Keep supported markets and optional service/catalog coverage in versioned configuration. App Store Connect controls distribution; backend adapters enforce approved service access. Client input alone cannot activate a disabled paid service. Missing shopping or image coverage leaves the independent closet, saved looks and recovery usable.
- Start with the United States selected explicitly; do not opt into all current/future storefronts automatically. Additional markets require current legal/privacy/processor/payment/support assessment, any needed localization and source/fit evidence before enablement under Section 24. App distribution does not itself verify shipping or size coverage.
- A supported country may be enabled through App Store Connect and compatible backend configuration without a new app architecture. New languages, screens, schemas, integrations or platform requirements can still require an app update. Do not promise that a switch alone resolves legal obligations or delivers untested features.
- Before rollout, test regional configuration, units/sizing, prices, service-unavailable states and any localized layouts on iPhone/iPad, including Lily's existing fit/color/workflow cases. Preserve her profile, entitlements, sponsored policy and simple primary flow. Broader clothing/audience support remains the separate Section 10.2 validation decision.

References checked October 3, 2026: [Apple App Store country/region availability](https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/manage-your-apps-availability/manage-availability-for-your-app-on-the-app-store/) and [age-rating setup and higher-rating overrides](https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/manage-app-information/set-an-app-age-rating/).

### 11.9 Local search, indexing and retained-preview records

Use a SearchIndex protocol/service independent of the chosen canonical persistence framework. Prototype a private local SQLite full-text index with deterministic normalizers, a versioned synonym vocabulary and facet tables as one candidate; SQLite/FTS capability, schema and atomic update behavior must be validated on supported OS versions before selection. This is not an assertion that SwiftData provides the required full-text behavior. No hosted search vendor or embedding model is selected.

Ordinary garment search/styling filters join current canonical suitcase membership and item state, deduplicated by garment ID; Add garments instead uses the explicitly labeled Main Closet management picker without changing the working/remembered source. Saved source filters join captured outfit/preview provenance, including manual saves, rather than current membership. Removing a member or container never hides otherwise retained historical looks; deleted container labels become Former suitcase and legacy unknown provenance remains unknown. Current membership is rechecked when deliberately applying history to a working source, not required merely to view/search it. Index these distinct current/captured source facets incrementally and rebuild them from validated canonical records without AI; an index snapshot never grants current eligibility. Scope names and queries remain private.

The canonical local records and retained image files remain authoritative. SearchDocument entries are rebuildable derivatives, never the ownership, saved-look or entitlement database. Each document has a stable source-type/source-ID identity, a source revision or immutable snapshot/output reference, normalized reviewed fields, provenance, linked canonical IDs, facet values and an index schema/version. Image documents reference existing assets; do not duplicate full-resolution images into the text index. A generated-output identity includes its stable job/output identity rather than only an image hash; duplicate delivery is idempotent while two intentionally distinct jobs remain distinct history.

- Incrementally index durable item, outfit, preview, collection, membership and keyword changes off the main UI path. Serialize index writers, journal pending source revisions and advance a committed watermark only after an index transaction succeeds. Canonical saves must not wait for a full rebuild or cloud operation. A low-storage/corrupt index failure preserves the canonical store and exposes status/retry.
- Run initial build and rebuilds in bounded batches with cancellation/pause and a resumable version marker. Build a replacement index and atomically switch after validation; do not clear live canonical records to repair search. Querying never starts a model call, body analysis or full-resolution image decode. Thumbnails use the existing bounded cache/decoder.
- Bind query parameters and escape supported phrase/prefix syntax rather than execute input as SQL. Bound query size, synonym/typo expansion, facet selections and page size; return a useful input error instead of uncontrolled work. Exact/name/alias/synonym/prefix weighting and facet combination are versioned deterministic rules, with matched-field provenance. Run Section 7.6's bounded possible-match fallback only as a labeled separate tier; preserve literal quoted searches and explicit filters/constraints. Never use reduced-term matches as complete-current-scope absence evidence or automatic stylist selection. No analytics event contains queries, names/aliases, keywords, result IDs, captions or clothing/body content.
- Recheck result references against current canonical ownership/availability, tombstones and deletion generation before presentation or action. The index can point to a still-retained historical snapshot, but cannot grant current eligibility. Suppress deleted-content matches immediately even if background cleanup is pending. While index revisions lag, disclose that state and use a bounded canonical-metadata fallback where possible; never infer verified insufficient inventory from incomplete search.
- Preserve immutable preview snapshots and their reviewed keyword provenance. Updating a current garment refreshes its current document and related-current links without silently rewriting earlier image snapshots. Search-linked snapshots retain archived/history content only where the user's retention choice permits it. Permanent deletion purges affected content from documents, keyword/caption copies and dependent opaque images under Sections 7.4/15; minimal content-free deletion markers remain.
- After migration, sync-conflict resolution or archive restore, derive indexing work from the surviving canonical revisions and deletion markers. Exclude the derivative index from authoritative exports; include manual keywords, collections, memberships, lineage and retained assets so a fresh installation can rebuild without any provider, paid generation or old Photos library. Restored indexes or late jobs cannot resurrect purged images/content.
- Before acknowledging a delivered preview as Kept, validate and durably commit its image/hash, composition snapshot and unique history link. Failed download/save remains explicit and resumes the same bounded job; do not re-run inference or claim durable history from an expiring provider URL. Favorites do not control this automatic retention. Accountless credits/sponsorship remain verified on the backend and cannot be restored from an image/history/archive record.

Personal name/alias/descriptor commits use stable canonical annotation IDs, provenance, deletion markers and per-item label revisions; incrementally index them and reconcile explicit removals/conflicts rather than unioning every old alias back into the item. Save only the reviewed phrase from Remember this name, never an automatic query log. Renaming changes current linked labels, not immutable captured preview facts. Keep presentation/search label changes separate from actual appearance-input revisions so they cannot invalidate intact exact image reuse or trigger provider work. Confirmed color/cut/source corrections follow the existing current-fact/version rules and retain earlier snapshots honestly. Rebuild names/aliases from surviving records after restore without an AI service.

For the initial-release search gate, use a physical iPhone 12 fixture with 1,000 garments, 500 saved/manual outfits and 1,500 retained previews plus their keywords/linked assets. This is a validation dataset, not a product cap. Target p95 query-to-first-correct-result metadata presentation of 300 ms or less after the latest index is ready, including keyword, phrase, prefix, synonym and facet queries, under documented normal conditions: release build, nominal thermal state, adequate free disk, no active migration/rebuild and bounded cached thumbnails. Measure at least 100 representative queries; separately report indexing delay, cold/index-rebuilding behavior and thumbnail availability instead of hiding them. Also test stressed import/sync and iPad adaptive/accessibility conditions; local work remains responsive and status truthful when this target is not applicable.

Advanced semantic search, image-to-image similarity or automatic pixel-derived garment recognition may be evaluated later behind separate local feasibility, rights, privacy, resource and any provider-cost gates. Do not label metadata/keyword image retrieval as validated visual similarity. Any later cloud feature requires its own deliberate action and approved consent; ordinary search must remain local and independently usable.

[SQLite's official FTS5 documentation](https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html) supports phrase/prefix queries and ranked full-text retrieval. Synonyms, domain normalization, facets and source-resolution behavior remain application design and prototype obligations; the source does not validate this app's iOS implementation or performance.

### 11.10 Provider-independent development and simulated services

Decision confirmed October 3, 2026: the complete planned experience must be developable and testable with fictional data and simulated AI/image results. Define versioned stylist and image-job contracts first, build the real screens and durable local workflows against them, and connect real adapters independently. Production provider selection is deferred until early comparisons establish useful behavior; live image feasibility proceeds alongside development rather than waiting for a finished app.

- Inject service implementations through defined interfaces. A scripted MockStylist uses the same typed outfit, swap and failure contracts; a separate MockImageProvider uses the same parent/child job, captured-version, delivery and cancellation contracts. Prebuilt permitted images represent simulated outputs, not proof of generated clothing fidelity. Use the actual history commits, file validation, search, drafts and editor paths rather than a disconnected demonstration UI.
- Add independent MockWebSearch and MockShoppingRanker behind the production contracts: public intent, genuine-source fixtures, per-field provenance, empty/failure/refusal, unsafe links/injection, stale variants and changed destination/profile/source. A mock URL is clearly fictional, not live availability. Exercise the real local purchase/arrival/reminder service with a controllable clock, notification permission states and restart/sync/import reconciliation; notification scheduling is distinct from the canonical reminder record.
- Supply resettable fictional profiles, wardrobe records, source photos, outfits, previews and preference histories, including large fixtures and older captured versions. Keep test stores separate from Lily's personal store. Clearly label the development environment and simulated imagery. Weather, remote discovery, entitlement and ledger services need deterministic test substitutes too, so UI exercises do not require live network, paid API access or a real purchase.
- Use a controllable clock and scripted completion order to exercise instant/slow success, one ready/one pending/one failed direction, malformed text, corrupt images, service rejection, uncertain paid dispatch, cancellation and delayed output. Simulate repeated taps/callbacks, relaunch, swaps or deletion while processing, full disk and interrupted saving. Verify dispatch/reservation/delivery counters without long real waits; mock financial records are never actual billing evidence.
- Mock-versus-real selection is an explicit development/test configuration, not a user-controlled production authorization bypass. Unknown client flags cannot activate mock entitlements or disable server admission. Fictional test inputs and captured diagnostic assertions must not leak into production analytics or user data.
- Record simulated test evidence separately from real integration evidence. Mocks can validate interaction, deterministic rules, durability and orchestration; they cannot establish actual model intelligence, body/garment/shoe fidelity, provider latency/cancellation, Apple purchase/sync behavior or physical fit. Validate real adapters, sandbox purchases, actual sync and applicable TO gates before claiming those services work.

### 11.11 Local styling history and reuse before inference

Decision confirmed October 3, 2026: check retained private history before paid AI, so a useful earlier look, explanation or matching preview can serve Lily again, including six months later. Reuse is an application feature independent of the selected AI providers. It does not guarantee that network traffic decreases continuously as her closet grows.

**History and privacy.** Commit versioned outfit/explanation records through the real durable local store, linked to canonical pieces, captured revisions, rule/schema/provider provenance and an allowlisted structured intent such as occasion, palette, starting item and owned-only mode. Reuse is scoped to the active user's private store, never a cross-user personal-answer cache. Preserve only reviewed context suitable for outfit history; today-only neutral clothing comfort is included only when she explicitly chooses to keep it with that look, never inherited as a later default or recorded as a symptom timeline. Do not store six months of raw chat, raw request text, symptoms/body-feel notes, or hashes/embeddings that substitute for that sensitive text. Retained explanations must pass the same allowed-content checks; echoed sensitive text is not retained simply because it came from an AI response. Unreviewed model tags are not confirmed facts. History remains personal data, with local editing/deletion, optional private CloudKit sync and versioned export/restore. No automatic six-month expiry applies. Scoped deletion removes covered explanations, descriptors, dependency records and derived indexes as well as images; deletion markers prevent resurrection.

**Matching and validation.** A local HistoryReuseService uses structured context and reviewed metadata; cloud semantic or pixel-similarity search is not required. Equal question wording alone never establishes an exact hit. An exact text/result hit requires all relevant typed constraints and dependencies to be represented and compatible with current schema/rules. If free text contains unresolved constraints, offer earlier candidates for review or a fresh request; never drop “avoid heels,” reinterpret unknown intent, or fingerprint raw sensitive prose to manufacture a hit. A similar-history match is a candidate, not an exact answer. Recheck current selected-source membership, current owned IDs, Dirty/Unavailable/Archived/Trash status, confirmed arrival for PurchasedConfirmed, explicit overrides, required colors, occasion, current weather/season, exclusions, reversible feedback and relevant confirmed fit/preference revisions. Unknown or stale weather stays labeled and follows Section 21's manual/failure fallback; an old forecast cannot become today's evidence. Recheck again when applying a look if records changed after retrieval. A saved appearance or rationale is never a fit guarantee.

Adding unrelated clothes need not make an earlier complete look unusable. Revalidate the selected pieces and relevant context rather than deleting all history whenever the closet changes. However, an earlier whole-generation answer, PartialWardrobe/InsufficientWardrobe result, missing-piece claim or versatility count depends on the current inventory scope. Recompute its current complete-scope evidence before presenting it as current; do not reuse an old negative result merely because its selected-item dependencies match. Keep captured Main Closet/suitcase provenance on history; membership changes do not erase it, but application must pass the current selected source. An unrelated garment outside that source need not invalidate a complete valid look. A valid earlier look is presented as history, without claiming a fresh evaluation of all new clothes. Rejected/“don't suggest again” looks remain searchable history but are excluded from automatic recommendations unless she deliberately revisits them; New ideas also respects recent-history novelty rules.

**User choice and charging.** Show a fully revalidated look as “From your history — still matches today's requirements,” with Use this look / Browse similar / New ideas. Otherwise show Earlier look or a specific changed/missing-context badge and allow review/manual editing. Local lookup, retained explanations and valid look application incur no new stylist/image units and remain available offline, after service expiry/quota exhaustion, or after withdrawal of cloud-processing permission where retained-content permission still allows them. New ideas deliberately bypasses text-result reuse and uses the normal authorized, metered stylist route. It does not automatically request new images; a separately enabled, disclosed On Me action still supplies image authorization. Never substitute a cache miss with silent paid regeneration.

**Images use exact retained-output matching.** Match ordered garment/shoe IDs and source-image versions, composition/outfit render inputs, reference asset/version, mode/coverage, render settings and compatible provider/model/configuration revision. Verify intact retained bytes and lawful retained-use permission. Favorite/title/collection/suitcase-name/membership/default-view changes alone do not alter render identity. Scope membership affects current outfit eligibility, not the pixels of an intact historical output. An older reference/source/configuration output remains viewable as its captured version, but does not satisfy a different current render request. Keep representative, approximate, unsupported and known-mismatch labels; a disliked or known unfaithful output is not automatically promoted as a faithful match. Check matching outputs before new-image quota/top-up/reservation admission. For three directions with two exact hits and one miss, show the two ready pictures and reserve customer units/provider dollars only for the admitted new child; any separate fresh text styling still has its normal treatment. Reuse links the original output, without duplicate GeneratedLook records, delivery debits or inferred ownership. Missing/corrupt assets offer bounded recovery/download from an existing permitted copy, manual alternatives or an explicit new request; they never trigger hidden inference. Update Preview checks exact reuse first. Regenerate intentionally bypasses output reuse and creates a new action, whose repeated taps/retries/callbacks remain idempotent.

**Separate caches and evidence.** Long-lived history is not a developer-hosted closet replica. Keep the gateway's normalized completed-action cache encrypted, principal-scoped and limited to 24 hours for recovery; preserve withdrawal/deletion fences. Provider prompt-prefix caching reuses request computation and still makes an API request, so consent, admission, metering and finite retries still apply; provider/model-specific terms must be verified before configuration. It is not a six-month answer archive. See [OpenAI's prompt caching documentation](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching). Repeat-use inference/image calls may fall, while weather refresh, sync, authentication and retained-asset transfers can still use the network. Measure content-free reuse/miss/dispatch counts only under existing diagnostics consent/allowlists; no raw questions, wardrobe identifiers or personal cache keys in telemetry. Test six-month reuse, changed circumstances, mixed hits, explicit freshness, recovery and deletion using Section 11.10's controllable clock and production application paths; all real integration/release checks remain Not run.

## 12. Core Data Model

| Entity | Key fields |
| --- | --- |
| UserProfile | Stable id/revision; optional confirmed height/measurements with units/basis/source/update/confirmation; optional contextual weight; category/brand sizing contexts; editable usual fit/style/color preferences and confirmed fit-priority criteria; chosen clothing/catalog preferences; budget; versioned preferred/avoided retailer pool and optional reviewed order, initially Amazon/TikTok Shop/Target/Walmart/H&M with other permitted sources allowed; independently confirmed shopping destination. Lily's height is confirmed; inseam remains unknown until confirmed. No height/weight/size/gender cutoff; adult-service eligibility remains separate under Section 24.2. |
| MeasurementValue / SizeContext | Typed dimension/value or explicit preferred length/range/unit; body/preferred-garment/actual-garment/chart basis; source, lastUpdated, confirmation and profile revision; raw size label with category/brand/sizing system and source chart/cut context. Preserve unknowns and distinctions; no height/photo-derived values. |
| WardrobeItem | Stable id/revision; optional displayName and personalAlias/reviewedDescriptor links with labelRevision; category or explicit Unknown, confirmed color/shade, silhouette, season, brand?, sizeContext?, fabric?, notes?, editable searchKeywords with provenance/confirmation; sourceAssetId?, activeDerivativeId?, imageRevision/source and distinct appearance-input revision; ownershipStatus/ownershipRevision with explicit transition basis, including NoLongerOwned; independent availability/availabilityRevision and previous non-Archived status where known; purchased actual variant? and independent arrivalState/revision/confirmation (NotArrived / Arrived / Unknown; unknown purchased arrival is ineligible); trashDate?/purgeAfter?, unavailableUntil?, sourceRetailer?/URL?, purchaseDate?. Labels do not establish garment facts; incomplete metadata permits manual save but cannot satisfy unknown hard styling constraints. Status/ownership-only edits preserve appearance inputs. AI proposals never overwrite confirmed fields silently. |
| GarmentAnnotation / MetadataProposal | Stable item-scoped annotation ID, kind (personal alias/reviewed descriptor), reviewed text, provenance, confirmation, revision/removal marker and optional source-asset reference; normalized lookup form is rebuildable. Optional understanding proposals capture source/item versions, provider/configuration and uncertainty with Accept/Edit/Skip status, kept separate from confirmed facts. Do not retain raw query transcripts/sensitive text, create global aliases, infer ownership or copy proposal strings into authoritative fields without review. |
| Suitcase / SuitcaseMembership | Stable container ID/name/metadata revision, archived state and deletion generation; unique garment/suitcase pair with stable membership ID, revision and removal marker. Multiple memberships are allowed; each links one canonical garment without copying records/assets. Removing/archiving/deleting the container preserves clothing and permitted looks/previews; permanent garment deletion removes its covered links. |
| WardrobeScope / WardrobeViewPreference | MainCloset or one Suitcase ID; captured working source, membership/context revision and eligible-inventory dependencies for requests/drafts/history; distinct resolved-complete, incomplete-recovery, unresolved-reference and confirmed-invalid readiness. Captured provenance does not assert current membership; unknown legacy provenance stays Unknown. Durable remembered latest deliberate selection, editable in Settings; initial choice Main Closet. Invalid confirmed archived/deleted preference resolves visibly to Main Closet, valid empty remains selected, unresolved sync reference preserves the pending preference. Persist fallback reason/awaiting explicit source choice separately from the display preference; a relaunch cannot authorize broader actions. A remote preference never silently rewrites an open action. No physical-location/GPS claim. |
| TourProgress / TourPreferences | Stable logical tour ID, last seen content version, outcome (Finished / Skipped / ControlActivated), and update date; default-on Show tips on new screens preference. Device-local preference records with normal preference export/import/private-sync handling, atomic writes and Delete My Data reset. Responsive layouts share IDs; in-session replay/step state is transient. No wardrobe content, body values, prompts or analytics identifier. See Section 7.8. |
| LaundryUpdateAction | Minimal private local recovery record: stable action/idempotency ID, individually selected or reviewed-all target IDs, explicit Entire closet / This suitcase scope and expected target membership revision/removal generation and container archive/deletion state where applicable, expected ownership/availability revisions, intended Dirty→Available transitions, committed/skipped counts and bounded compound-undo references. No whole-closet future command, wash calendar, inference authorization or financial debit. Retry/undo rechecks current revisions and deletion; follow existing bounded recovery/export/conflict rules rather than keep an indefinite laundry transcript. |
| DailyStyleContext / ColorConstraint | Request-scoped occasion/date/destination/weather source/conditions; optional neutral clothing-comfort/ease preference; required versus preferred color, confirmed shade vocabulary, affected piece/portion scope and other explicit hard/soft constraints. No inferred cycle/body state or symptom timeline. Raw symptom/cycle/body-feel free text and unreviewed temporary context are excluded from automatic persistence, sync and indexing. Only an explicitly reviewed neutral clothing preference, such as relaxed waist, may accompany a draft/saved look when the user chooses to retain it; it does not automatically apply to future requests. Searchable clothing labels require that same review; no sensitive timeline or content analytics. |
| StyleRequest | Stable action/idempotency ID; profile revision and fit-rule version/context; DailyStyleContext; weather snapshot; season; selected startingItem IDs; bounded transient freeText; initiating draft/action context revision and declared inventory-readiness evidence; owned-only/Suggestions mode, with owned-only required whenever WardrobeScope is a suitcase; explicit shopping permission; target count three and approved lane meanings; WardrobeScope and captured membership/context revisions; eligible wardrobe snapshot/revision and individual availability overrides; unchanged piece IDs/layout for a swap.
